# Bibliography

*Every external work this project cites, reproduces, depends on, or corrects has an entry here, added the day it
is first used (Kooshi, 2026-09-06). Each entry records **(i)** what of ours rests on it, **(ii)** where our copy
is, and **(iii)** how much of it has actually been read — by section wherever the read was partial. A theorem in
`CLAUDE.md` § Frontier, or a grokking, that rests on prior art names its entry by the key in column one of § A.*

*This file states current state only. A corrected claim is replaced by the corrected claim, with its
attribution; how each entry was reached — the dated amendments, corrections and struck-out claims — is in git.
The last version carrying that history is commit `f1c78f4` (`git show f1c78f4:BIBLIOGRAPHY.md`).*

*Copies live in `~/source/library/`, outside this repository; copy paths below are relative to `~/source/`.
`library/papers/` holds Kooshi's downloads, `library/pdf/` the search agent's and later fetches,
`library/notes/` text extractions, search findings; the one book sits at the top
level. § B lists every held file with its key and read status.*

*Markers.* **[title from a search listing]** — the title was seen only in web-search results and has not been
checked against the paper. **[title unverified]** (or **[unverified]**) — the description is ours, not the paper's. Rows marked
*(analysis ✓)* or *(analysis ~)* came from the web-Claude session's analysis (`reference/Chat-analysis/`,
2026-09-06), carrying that session's confidence mark, and are second-hand here unless the row says otherwise.
Read status: **read in full**; **read** with the parts named; **scanned** (abstract, introduction, results and
reference list, or the parts named); **abstract**; **second-hand** (content known through another work, which the
row names); **not read**. **Held** means a copy is in `~/source/library/`.

## R. Outstanding requests — papers we need and do not hold

*For Kooshi, who files these. Current state only: a paper that arrives leaves this section the same day (its row in § A gets the
file path and an honest read-status). Last revised 2026-09-20. Everything here was checked against the zbMATH Open API on
2026-09-19/20 unless a row says otherwise; who holds which volumes  are in the library's notes §§ "How to file it", 4 and 5.*

**When one arrives:** save it under `~/source/library/papers/` named `author-year-short-title-venue-pages.pdf`, tell the session,
and the session reads it, mines its reference list into this file the same day (§ D rule), and clears the row here.

## A. Load-bearing — works our results reproduce, depend on, or correct

| key | work | what of ours rests on it | copy | read |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **[deGrey2018]** | A. D. N. J. de Grey, *The chromatic number of the plane is at least 5*, Geombinatorics 28 (2018) 18–31; arXiv:1804.02385 | rung B (the 1,581-vertex witness, kernel-certified, M06–M09); the anatomy G1–G10, G27, G31; the forced pairs of the 791-forcer | `library/pdf/deGrey2018-chromatic-number-plane-at-least-5.pdf` (arXiv:1804.02385v3, 30 May 2018; saved 2026-09-25 from a scratch copy) | read (earlier sessions) |
| **[Heule2018]** | M. J. H. Heule, *Computing small unit-distance graphs with chromatic number 5*, Geombinatorics 28 (2018); arXiv:1805.12181 | the 553- and 510-vertex witnesses, kernel-certified; G6, G13 | not held | read (earlier sessions) |
| **[Heule2019]** | M. J. H. Heule, *Trimming graphs using clausal proof optimization*, arXiv:1907.00929 (the 529-vertex graph) | Four findings bear on us, and two of them are our own results seen from outside. **(i) §5, titled "Observed patterns of points in `Q[√3,√11] × Q[√3,√11]`", names our field `K` in 2019**, and states that the key component of `G553` is 420 points in `K²`. His generators are the radius-`1/3` hexagon `H_{1/3}` and its 90°-rotated copy `H'_{(√3+√11)/6}`, whose squared radius `(7+√33)/18` lies in `ℚ(√33)` — G3/G4's "every exact radius is a seam radius", confirmed at source rather than measured. **(ii) §6.2 states pigeonhole × kill as a design principle, attributed to `[Heule2018]`**: *"the large part limits the number of valid 4-coloring of 12 vertices at distance 2 from the origin to 19. The small part prevents these 12 vertices to having any of these 19 4-colorings."* The large part is the `K`-object, hence 4-colourable by Theorem B, so the kill must leave `K` — **G15/G16's "the field wall is exactly the wall between the two acts", legible in the construction's own description.** **(iii) `G2167` is a `K`-object, and the colouring regularities he observes on it include two instances of the 2-adic law (ledger row 59), which he records as unexplained**: points `1/3` apart get different colours (a 2-adic unit — a non-mono-pair is permitted and a mono-pair barred) and points `2/3` apart get the same colour (valuation 1 — exactly the reverse). **(iv) "Virtual edge" is his published term** — *"Hence 1/3 is a so-called virtual-edge in 4-colorings of unit-distance graphs"* — so the coinage is the SAT/Polymath community's and not ours (§ E). Free data: `G529` ships with points, edges, a CNF and a seconds-long refutation proof at `github.com/marijnheule/CNP-SAT`; and *"we constructed several graphs with up to 100 000 vertices, but all were 5-colorable"* — an external null for the 6-hunt, beside G10/G31. **G8's "shrinking scars" reading is neither confirmed nor refuted**: `G2167`'s generators are `ζ₁₂`-words, so the out-of-group residue directions of 510/553 would have to come from the small part inherited from the `G553` lineage — a cheap tools check, not run | `library/pdf/arXiv-1907.00929-heule-529-trimming.pdf` | **read in full (2026-09-09, FS-23)** |
| **[ElZaharSauer1985]** | M. El-Zahar, N. Sauer, *The chromatic number of the product of two 4-chromatic graphs is 4*, Combinatorica 5(2) (1985) 121–126, doi 10.1007/BF02579374 [title, venue and doi confirmed by Crossref, 2026-09-16; the paper itself unread] | cited by GPT Astra's Theorem 3.1 (`reference/ChatGPT-local-global-tightness-conjecture/local-global-further-results.md` §3.4; FS-46, `plans/STUB-three-colour-classification.md`) for one fact: a categorical product of graphs each needing four colours needs four (Hedetniemi's conjecture at four colours), so a three-colourable product `∏ UD(𝔽_{q_j})` has a three-colourable factor — the step that turns "every three-colouring of `F²` descends to `R/3R`" into "some prime of `F` has residue field `𝔽₃`". Nothing of ours is derived from the paper yet; FS-46's PA phase reads it. Contrast `[Tardif2023]`: the conjecture fails at every higher level (the product of two 5-chromatic graphs can be 4-chromatic), so three colours is its last true level (`[Zhu2025]` §3); it decides FS67-W's and DB-1's attribution (FS-67's gate, `plans/FS67-PA-close.md` Stage 5) | not held | not read |
| **[ExooIsmailescu2019]** | G. Exoo, D. Ismailescu, *The chromatic number of the plane is at least 5 — a new proof*, Discrete Comput. Geom. (2019), doi 10.1007/s00454-019-00058-1; arXiv:1805.00157 | It corrects one ledger row and poses two of the questions this project tracks. (a) **Their Claim 2.1 is T20's mechanism, in 2018.** A 40-point graph `G40` in `Q[√3,√11]` whose *"crucial property … is that for any proper 4-coloring which avoids monochromatic pairs distance √(11/3) apart, vertices [0,0,0,0] and [0,0,96,0] are colored the same"* — those two vertices are at distance **8/3** — then *"rotate G40 around vertex [0,0,0,0] until the new position of [0,0,96,0] is distance 1 apart from its original location"*, i.e. by `ρ_{8/3}`, and *"since sin θ = 3√247/128 … all the 79 points lie in Q[√3,√11,√247]"*. **Same field, same spindle rotation, same forced-pair-then-spindle move as M53's T20.** Their forced pair is CONDITIONAL (on no mono `√(11/3)`-pair), so the spindle yields a pigeonhole rather than `χ ≥ 5`, and their proof needs three acts — *"our method is less efficient as it employs three steps"*. **Ours: the UNCONDITIONAL forced pair at 8/3 on K-fabric (the 588-core), hence χ ≥ 5 in one act, and the exact value χ(K(√247)²) = 5 (M58), which they do not have. Not ours: T20 is not the first 5-forcer without √5 or √7.** (b) Concluding section, verbatim: *"whether there exists a 5-chromatic unit distance graph which can be embedded in Q[√3,√11] × Q[√3,√11]. If true, this would be the best possible as a recent result of Madore shows [5]. More generally, what is the smallest subfield of E² that requires 5 colors?"* — **an independent posting of the question Theorem B answers**, in the strongest venue among its postings; the second half is M58's sieve, open. (c) Three lines on: *"What is the minimum order of a 5-chromatic unit distance graph in the plane?"* — a posted open question, and the one the word "minimal" in any mascot programme is about. (d) Also on that page: Heule's 826-vertex witness in `Q[√3,√11,√5]`. (e) Their [5] is `[Madore2015]`, three lines above (b) — so a walk of Madore's citations reaches this question (FS-20's prior-art repair, `plans/STUB-theorem-B-writeup.md` §1). **Row 121 answers its smallest-subfield question in part** (ℚ(√47) is the first real quadratic field needing five) | `library/pdf/arXiv-1805.00157-exoo-ismailescu.pdf` | **read (2026-09-09, Coxeter): §2 Claim 2.1, concluding section, reference list** |
| **[ExooIsmailescu2020]** | G. Exoo, D. Ismailescu, *A 6-chromatic two-distance graph in the plane*, Geombinatorics 29 (2020); arXiv:1909.13177; data at cs.indstate.edu/ge/ExooIsmailescuData/ | **the seed of the descent** (M49–M53): the 214-point {1, 2}-graph H with 446 distance-2 edges forcing (205, 206) at distance 5, re-derived exactly (M49); the calibration bed of M54 (C(H) = ∅, ν(H) = 38); **M56: its 35 five-colourings against the additive family** — their §2 builds H from a 23-point set S in the frame [a, b, c, d] = ((a√3 + b√11)/12, (c + d√33)/12) by the two axis reflections and the π/3 rotations plus nine extra vertices, and §3 counts the colourings (18 for G, 35 for H) by exhaustive search; **no algebraic colouring, no modular/additive argument, Fischer not cited** — the ℤ/5-additivity of the forced pair (M56 §R2) is not in the paper. Whether later literature has it is not checked: `[Soifer2024]`'s Exoo–Ismailescu chapter (ch. 58) was in FS-18's targeted read, which looked for number-field and finite-field content, not for this | `library/papers/arXiv-1909.13177-exoo-ismailescu-6chromatic-two-distance.pdf` | read (M49; §2–§3 re-read 2026-09-06 for M56) |
| **[Parts2020a]** | J. Parts, *Graph minimization, focusing on the example of 5-chromatic unit-distance graphs in the plane*, arXiv:2010.12665 (Geombinatorics 29/4); the 509-vertex record | The vertex-count record every "smaller witness" claim is measured against; the paper M63 reproduces; import target (G3/G4 falsifier). **The record** is `G = L ∪ ρS`, a large and a small subgraph sharing ONE vertex: **`509 = 374 + 136 − 1`**, stated in three places — §6's running text (*"a large subgraph with 374 vertices and 1860 edges and a small subgraph with 136 vertices and 564 edges"*), Table 1's L and S rows, and Tables 3 and 4, whose orbit fillings sum to exactly 374 and 136. Heule's 553 = 420 + 134 and his 529 = 393 + 137 — **so the symmetric `2n − 1` spindle cost model is wrong for the record**. Moorhouse, *On the Chromatic Numbers of Planes*, preliminary draft revised 3 March 2010, `ericmoorhouse.org/pub/chromatic.pdf` (unpublished as far as searches show, and little cited) | **Major prior art for M58–M61.** §1 defines `K²` for *any field or commutative ring with unity* by `(a−c)² + (b−d)² = 1` — our `unitQuadrance R` (M58). §3.1 and §4.2(i): `χ(K²) = χ(K₀²)` for the component of the origin, with the `ℚ²` component named (translates of `ℤ_S[i]`, `S` the primes ≡ 1 mod 4) — the same fact as `[Fischer1990]`'s Example 1. §4.2(ii) is `LocalGlobal.addCayleyMap` / `colorable_of_addCayleyMap` (M61). **§8.2 Lemma and §10.3 Theorem are THE FINITE-PLANE BOUND** — "there exists a graph homomorphism `K² → 𝔽_q²` so that `χ(K²) ≤ χ(𝔽_q²)`" for any number field `K` and any prime with `\|O/𝔭\| ≡ 3 (mod 4)`, by exactly our proof (unit vectors are `𝔭`-integral — his Lemma 2.3; the denominator is outside `𝔭` because `−1` is not a square in the residue field; reduce). **§6 Table 6.1 gives `χ(𝔽₁₁²) = 5`**, with `χ(𝔽₃²) = 3`, `χ(𝔽₇²) = 4`, `χ(𝔽₉²) = 3`, `χ(𝔽₁₃²) ∈ {5,6}` (this project measured 5 at the finite-plane pivot), `χ(𝔽₁₇²) ∈ {5,6,7}` (still open, untouched here), and the guess that `χ(𝔽_q²) → ∞` (settled by `[Vinh2005]`, which he does not cite — the two literatures did not meet). §7.1: odd-degree number fields have `χ(K²) = 2`, by a residue colouring at a prime above 2 through the trace of `𝔽_{2^f}` — the same 2-adic mechanism as Theorem B, and a special case of FS-36's two-colour theorem (a prime above 2 with `e` and `f` odd ramifies in `K(i)`, and the trace is one functional that is `1` at `1`). §9: composite ideals cannot beat the minimum over primes without refuting **Hedetniemi's Conjecture**. **§1, the headline for us: he identifies `ℚ(√3, √11)` as the smallest number field whose plane contains a Moser spindle (Prop. 1.4(b): iff `K` holds `1/66`, `√3`, `√11`), gets `χ ≥ 4`, and writes "We have not determined the exact value of `χ(K²)` in this case." Theorem B answers that question.** Ledger rows 52–53 and 56 (the pivot's bound, `χ(UD(𝔽₁₁,2)) = 5`, `unitQuadrance` over a ring, `addCayleyMap`) have prior art here and are not new mathematics; the kernel certificates remain ours and appear to be the first. Not in it: Theorem B, the 2-adic wall criterion, the field sieve (M58), the lift theory (M59), the compulsion transfer (M60) and the tightness mechanism (M61). **§8 (re-read 2026-09-15, FS-53 §PA): Theorem 8.1 — real quadratic `ℚ(√d)` has `χ ≤ 4` unless `d ≡ 47, 59, 83 (mod 84)` (the sieve at 2, 3, 7; 11 unused); Lemma 8.2 counts a ramified `p` (`d ≡ 0 mod p`) as usable; Theorem 8.6, `√p ∈ K` with `p ≡ 3 (mod 4)` gives an odd cycle from Pell's equation. Table 6.1's two bracketed cells, `χ(𝔽₁₃²) ∈ {5, 6}` (5 here, M58) and `χ(𝔽₁₇²) ∈ {5, 6, 7}` (open; five unfinished here at 50 min), are the only bracketed finite planes in print — both at `p ≡ 1 (mod 4)`, sealing no field (FS-53 phase A′)** **FS-66 (§8.4–§9 re-read first-hand 2026-09-20): §9 writes the bound with prime-power ideals, `χ(K²) ≤ χ((O/B)²) ≤ min_j χ((O/𝔭_j^{e_j})²)`, noting every ideal he used was prime or the square of a prime (Theorem 8.5, the ramified 2); whether `e_j ≥ 2` can beat `e_j = 1` at an odd prime is not asked.** **§7.1 is FS54-T2's prior art** (odd degree ⟹ 2-colourable; rows 70, 77; `CLAUDE.md` G56). the upper bound `χ(ℚ(√11)²) ≤ 4` is kernel-checked since FS131-LU1 (`UpperHalf.chromaticNumber_planeOver_eleven`). | `library/papers/moorhouse-2010-chromatic-numbers-of-planes.pdf` | **read in full (2026-09-08); §6, §8 re-read 2026-09-15; § 8 re-read in full, FS131-PAS1**  |

| **[Krebs2022]** | M. Krebs, *Clique numbers of finite unit-quadrance graphs*, **Journal of Algebraic Combinatorics 57(1) (2023) 1–20**, doi `10.1007/s10801-022-01157-8`, published online 16 Aug 2022, ISSN 0925-9899 / 1572-9192, sole author (Dept. of Mathematics, California State University, Los Angeles). Sections: Introduction · Bipartiteness · A parametrization of the generating set in two dimensions · Connectedness · Clique numbers · Chromatic numbers. *(Record confirmed against Crossref 2026-09-08: 16 references, cited once.)* | Named by the Poros session; citation verified. **Determines `ω(UD(F, n))` completely, for every finite field `F` and every `n`** (Theorems 1.2, 1.3), decides when these graphs are bipartite and when connected, and bounds their chromatic numbers. **Theorem 5.2, for any field `F` and not only finite ones: `ω(UD(F,2)) = 2` if `char F = 2` or 3 is not a square in `F`, and `3` otherwise**; for `p ≡ 3 (mod 4)` that is `ω = 3` iff `p ≡ 11 (mod 12)` and `ω = 2` iff `p ≡ 7 (mod 12)`. It agrees six for six with the values this project holds — kernel `decide`s at `p = 11` (3) and `p = 19` (2), and M61's measurements at 23, 31, 43, 47 (3, 2, 2, 3) — **so the clique number of every finite Euclidean plane is 2 or 3, in closed form**, sharper than `[Vinh2006]`'s `ω ≤ 4`; it retires M59's per-prime computation. The same closed form follows by specialising `[LorenzZimmermann2023]`'s Theorem 4.4 to `q = ⟨1,1⟩`, `a = 1`, `n = 2` (binary forms over a finite field are isometric iff their discriminants agree mod squares; `⟨1,1⟩` has Gram determinant 4 and the `A₂` test form 3, so they are isometric iff 3 is a square) — that specialisation is ours. **His entire `χ > ω` content is Proposition 6.1: if `ω ∤ q^n` then `χ ≥ ω + 1`** — a counting bound (translates of a maximum independent set by an `ω`-clique are disjoint, so `α ≤ q^n/ω`, strict when `ω ∤ q^n`, and `q^n/χ ≤ α`). **It is WEAKER than what this project holds at every prime we care about**: at `p = 11` it gives `χ ≥ 4` where the kernel has `χ = 5`; at 19 it gives `χ ≥ 3` where we have 5; at 23 it gives `χ ≥ 4` where we have `≥ 5`. **So G24/G40/G44's gap question is not settled in the literature and stays ours**, and the acquisition item under FS-13 is discharged with a negative. His Example 6.2 credits `[Madore2015]` for `χ(UD(F,2)) ≥ 4` whenever 3 and 11 are both squares, and improves it for finite fields (11 superfluous). Remark 2.3 quotes `[Fischer1990]`'s Theorem 8 **with the wrong congruence** — *"N is not congruent to 2 (mod 3)"*, which is Theorem 9's hypothesis; Theorem 8's is `N ≢ 3 (mod 4)` — and **poses a question in writing**: *"It might be interesting, however, to try to find necessary and sufficient conditions for UD(F,n) to be bipartite for such fields"* (characteristic zero). FS-36's multiquadratic two-colour criterion, a hand theorem, answers it for real multiquadratic `F` at `n = 2` (`plans/FS36-multiquadratic-decomposition.md` §PA). Cites Benda–Perles, Chilakamarri, Fischer, Johnson, Madore, Medrano et al., Soifer, Vinh, Woodall — **the only paper read here that cites both literatures**. Krebs is also a co-author of `[CervantesKrebs2023]`. **Closed access**: Unpaywall reports `oa_status: closed`, there is no repository copy and no arXiv version, and zbMATH withholds its review; Kooshi obtained the PDF from Springer directly (a scripted fetch stops at a JavaScript client challenge) | `library/papers/krebs-2022-clique-numbers-unit-quadrance.pdf` | **read in full (2026-09-08)** |
| **[SchneiderSilva2013]** | C. Schneider, A. Silva, *Cliques and colorings in generalized Paley graphs and an approach to synchronization*, arXiv:1306.6050; J. Algebra Appl. (2015), doi 10.1142/S0219498815500887 | Named by the Poros session; citation verified. Studies generalized Paley graphs whose clique and chromatic numbers **coincide**, and links that to synchronization in 1-dimensional primitive affine permutation groups. Relevant because **`UD(𝔽_q, 2)` IS a generalized Paley graph** when `q ≡ 3 (mod 4)`: identifying `𝔽_q²` with `𝔽_{q²}` makes the quadrance the norm, so the connection set is the norm-one subgroup of order `q + 1` — the Cayley graph `GP(q², q − 1)` in the index convention. Our planes are exactly the `ω ≠ χ` case (`ω = 3`, `χ = 5` at `q = 11`), and the paper names a discipline — synchronization / primitive permutation groups — not yet on Kooshi's ladder under G30. **Theorem 4.7(3): for `Γ_{p²,m}`, `ω = χ ⟺ m ∣ (p+1)`.** Our plane `UD(𝔽_p,2)` is `Γ_{p²,m}` with `m = (p²−1)/(p+1) = p−1`, and `(p−1) ∣ (p+1) ⟺ (p−1) ∣ 2 ⟺ p ∈ {2,3}` — so **`ω ≠ χ` for EVERY `p ≥ 5`: no finite Euclidean plane above `p = 3` is perfect.** This is a clean group-theoretic proof of `[Vinh2006]`'s Theorem 4, whose printed proof leans on an erroneous spectral bound (see that row). Theorem 4.6 adds a rational-eigenvalue obstruction: for a vertex- and edge-transitive graph with `ω = χ = k`, `(k−1) ∣ degree` and `λ_min = −degree/(k−1)`. And **Proposition 4.5(4) — `Γ` regular and edge-transitive ⟹ `ϑ(Γ) = −nλ_n/(λ₁ − λ_n)` — is M61's "`ϑ` = Hoffman by arc-transitivity" derivation, published 2013** (from `[Lovasz1979]` Thm 9, which ledger row 56 credits). Bears on G24/G40/G44: the `χ`-versus-`ω` gap on our planes is a published fact, though not quantified | `library/pdf/arXiv-1306.6050-schneider-silva.pdf` | **read — main results (2026-09-08, FS-18)** |

Leyva, *Chromatic numbers of rank-two abelian Cayley graphs*, arXiv:2511.03028 (2025) | Of FS-18's prediction P4 — that this subsumes M61's periodic quotient — the mechanism half holds and the instrument half does not. A connected abelian Cayley graph is encoded by a **Heuberger matrix** (`m` rows = dimension, `r` columns = rank), and their Lemma 2.10(3) — *collapsing two rows by adding them is a graph homomorphism* — is the row-operation calculus that gives `χ` upper bounds. **The MECHANISM does subsume M61's periodic quotient**: a quotient homomorphism to a smaller Cayley graph. But our `LocalGlobal.addCayleyMap` (an additive hom whose kernel misses the connection set) is the **more general statement**, of which their row operations are instances — neither paper has the other's generality. **It is NOT a stronger instrument for our objects**: they completely determine `χ` only for dimension 1, rank 1, and dimension ≤ 3 with rank ≤ 2, and write *"the cases `r = 2, m ≥ 4` as well as `r ≥ 3` are left for future investigation"* — while `UD(𝔽_p,2) = Cay(𝔽_p², circle)` has rank `(p+1)/2` (6 at `p = 11`, 66 at `p = 131`). **The matrix method as it stands cannot compute `χ` of any finite Euclidean plane.** Two things worth keeping: Lemma 2.11 — `χ = 2` iff every column sum is even — is `[Fischer1990]`'s Theorem 3 in Cayley language; and **Example 2.14, a criterion in terms of the MINIMAL POLYNOMIAL of a rotation**: for `W = Cay(ℂ, {ω^n : n ≥ 0})` with `p = min_ℤ ω`, `χ(W) ≥ 4 ⟹ \|p(1)\| = \|p(−1)\| = 1` — the mirror of `[Fischer1990]`'s Theorem 7, and directly about the ladder objects of M24–M26. **Their stated open question, verbatim: "Does there exist an algebraic number `ω ∈ ℂ` such that the Cayley graph of `ℂ` generated by the non-negative powers of `ω` is not 3-colorable?"** They cite `[Vinh2005]`; they do not cite Moorhouse or Fischer **Example 2.13 (read 2026-09-15): the chromatic number of the infinite unit-distance Cayley graph in ℝ² generated by `1, ω, ω², ω³`, `ω = (5 + i√39)/8` — an infinite object, no finite plane; the paper cites `[Vinh2005]` for Hoffman-bound lower bounds and computes none of its graphs** | `library/pdf/arXiv-2303.06262-cervantes-krebs.pdf`, `…-2.pdf`, `…-rank2.pdf` | **read (2026-09-08, FS-18)** |

(viii) §5–§7: algebraically and real closed fields, changing the quadratic form, and the axiom of choice. **The one place he stops is where Theorem A begins:** in Proposition 3.9 he needs a subgroup that is *not a ring* (`(1/√2)A`), does the coset construction by hand, and writes the parenthetical *"which the author has not found the courage to try to formulate under the most general auspices"*. **Theorem A is that general formulation** — an arbitrary graph drawn in an abelian group, edge differences in a subgroup, a partial homomorphism out of it — and it is kernel-checked. Manta, *Triangle colorings require at least seven colors*, arXiv:1909.02708; Discrete Math. 344(7):112411 (2021) | **It does NOT subsume our cell family**, though `[SokolovVoronov2025]`'s one-line gloss of it reads as if it might. A *polygon colouring* is a proper colouring of `ℝ²` partitioned into uniformly coloured open polygonal regions, borders and vertices, locally finite, adjacent regions differing. **Theorem 2.2: a polygon colouring with a vertex of DEGREE ≥ 4 needs at least 7 colours.** Theorem 2.3: every triangle colouring has such a vertex, hence needs 7. **Our cell family is the excluded case**: the nearest-point cells of a triangular lattice are hexagons meeting three at a vertex — degree 3 everywhere — so Manta's hypothesis fails on exactly the colourings M37–M43 study. | **[Fischer1990b]** | K. G. Fischer, *The connected components of the graph `Q(√N₁, …, √N_d)²`*, Congressus Numerantium 72 (1990) 213–221 — the volume is the *Proceedings of the Twentieth Southeastern Conference on Combinatorics, Graph Theory, and Computing* (Boca Raton, 1989; zbMATH 0733.05048); manuscript dated March 1989 | **Prior art for the unit-vector ring, in the multiquadratic and quadratic cases — read in full 2026-09-16.** Σ₀ (his symbol) is the set of finite sums of unit vectors of `ℚ(√N₁,…,√N_d, i)`, a ring; every component of the plane is a translate of it (§2, §3 — **the component lemma**, stated in passing). **Lemma 1:** if x² + y² = c²p² with x, y ∈ ℤ[√N₁,…,√N_d], p ≡ 3 (mod 4) and every N_j a quadratic residue mod p, then p divides every coefficient of x and y (via the 2^d × 2^d conjugation matrix, determinant a power of 2) — **the p-integrality of unit vectors at a non-split prime, the integrality step of the residue bound, for multiquadratic fields**; no colouring is drawn from it. **Theorem 1:** for p ≡ 3 (mod 4) with (p, N_j) = 1 for all j, **1/p ∈ Σ₀ iff some −N_j is a quadratic residue mod p** — i.e. iff no conjugation-fixed prime of F(i) lies above p: **prime inversion (`CLAUDE.md` G55) for multiquadratic fields at odd unramified primes**, both directions, the "if" by an explicit sum of two unit vectors; Remark 2: 1/p ∈ Σ₀ for every p ≡ 1 (mod 4). **Corollary:** infinitely many components (completing J. Zaks's investigation, cited as a preprint). **Theorem 2:** √N_j ∈ Σ₀. **Theorem 3** (quadratic): for p ≡ 3 (mod 4), 1/p ∈ Σ₀ iff −N is a quadratic residue mod p (so never at a ramified p). **Theorem 4** (quadratic): **1/2 ∈ Σ₀ iff N ≡ 7 (mod 8)** — `CLAUDE.md` G35's "the wall is broken iff 1/2 ∈ ℤ[U]" for real quadratic fields. **Lemma 3:** a reduced unit vector ((a + bi)(x + y√N i))/c has x² + y²N ≢ 3, 6, 7 (mod 8); an even denominator is ≡ 2 (mod 4) unless N ≡ 7 (mod 8); an even denominator exists iff N ≢ 5 (mod 8) (printed "N ≢ 1 mod 5", a typo — the proof is the mod-8 case). **Theorem 5:** Σ₀ for every real quadratic field in closed form, as ℤ_S⁴ plus half-integer cosets by N mod 8 (S the primes p ≡ 1 (mod 4) and the p ≡ 3 (mod 4) with −N a residue; S* adds 2 when N ≡ 7 (mod 8)); the proof of (iv) prints "N = 1 mod 8" for 5 mod 8. **No colouring result anywhere**: the introduction cites `[Johnson1987]` and `[Fischer1990]` for chromatic numbers and notes Theorem 4 "properly follows" from additive colourings. **Consequences here:** (1) FS-20's item R1 closes with no competing χ(ℚ(√3,√11)²) = 4 — Theorem B's novelty is unaffected; (2) G55's prime inversion is Fischer's in the multiquadratic unramified-odd case and the quadratic case — GPT Astra's general form (every number field, every prime, by lying-over) remains the general statement, and M56 L7's "NOT claimed" conjecture was this theorem; (3) FS-47's registered hunch P3 ("prime inversion is not in Fischer") is lost; (4) Theorem 5 is a ready-made statement for FS-47's quadratic inhabitants. Obtained as a library copy Cited by `[Johnson2000]` as its [7] (p. 177): *"Fischer [7] goes further by showing that (ℚ²(√N₁, …, √N_t), 1) has infinitely many components for any positive integers N₁, …, N_t. He also characterizes the component of the origin … in (ℚ²(√N), 1), for any positive integer N"*. | `library/papers/fischer-1990-connected-components-Q-sqrtN1-sqrtNd-congr-numer-72-pp213-221.pdf` (scan with OCR; formulas read from the page images) | **read in full** (2026-09-16): every page; Lemma 1, Theorems 1–5 and Lemma 3 checked against the images, and Theorem 1's two directions re-derived |
| **[Johnson1987]** | P. D. Johnson Jr., *Two-colorings of real quadratic extensions of `ℚ²` that forbid many distances*, Congressus Numerantium 60 (1987) 51–58 — the *Proceedings of the Eighteenth Southeastern Conference on Combinatorics, Graph Theory, and Computing* (Boca Raton, 1987) (title and conference per zbMATH 0648.05006) | **Its main theorem, verbatim from the zbMATH review (Zbl 0648.05006): "Suppose that `m` is a squarefree positive integer, and `m ≡ 1 mod 4` or `m ≡ 2 mod 4`. Then `[ℚ(√m)]²` is two-colorable so that all the distances `√(p/q)`, `p, q` odd positive integers, are forbidden."** The review also confirms the method is an odd-cycle / bipartiteness criterion in a Cayley graph — the same shape as `[Johnson1990]` — so the step to "colour by the residue of a homomorphism" is not his either. His condition `m ≡ 1, 2 (mod 4)` is exactly `[Fischer1990]`'s `N ≢ 3 (mod 4)` for squarefree `N`, which is why Fischer cites him: this is Fischer's reference [8], credited with proving *"in more generality the 'if' portion"* of his Theorem 8 — the points of `ℚ(√N)²` for `N ≢ 3 (mod 4)` split into two classes realizing neither distance 1 **nor any `√(p/q)` with `p, q` odd**, i.e. **no distance of 2-adic valuation zero**, which is our residue colouring's forbidden set at the prime above 2. The direct ancestor of Theorem B's distance law. **Acquisition:** the author, or an interlibrary loan; Penn State and the University of Michigan are confirmed holders of volume 60 (HathiTrust search-only scans made from both copies). Brief: item [5] of the library's notes. **Cited by `[Johnson2006]` as its [11] under a shorter title, *Coloring real quadratic extensions of ℚ²*** (read 2026-09-27); p. 212 states its theorem as *"if m ≡ 1 or 2 mod 4, then there is a 2-coloring of (ℚ(√m))² = ℚ²(√m) which forbids all the distances √(p/q), p, q odd positive integers"*, and p. 213 its method: *"simply to find, or to show the existence of, the requisite homomorphism from ⟨S_D⟩ into ℤ₂"*. Also `[Johnson2000]`'s [12] (p. 176). | not held (conference proceedings) | **not read — main theorem known verbatim from the zbMATH review; content through `[Fischer1990]` §Thm 8 and `[Payne2009]`** |

Fischer, *Additive k-colorable extensions of the rational plane*, Discrete Math. 82 (1990) 181–195, doi 10.1016/0012-365X(90)90324-B | **The framework of the field-wall theorem** (additive colourings = our residue colourings): Theorem 1 (2-colourable ⟺ ring homomorphism Σ₀ → ℤ/2), Theorem 6 (transcendental extensions), **Theorems 8–10: the complete real-quadratic classification** — 2 colours iff N ≢ 3 (mod 4), 3 colours for N ≢ 2 (mod 3) hence χ(ℚ(√3)²) = χ(ℚ(√7)²) = 3, 4 colours for N ≡ 3 (mod 8), no additive k ≤ 6 for N ≡ 23 (mod 24). The field-wall note's Corollary 6.5 was corrected against his Theorem 8 (T23); what stays ours: the local criterion, the multiquadratic case, Theorem B's exact 4. **M56's L7 is his Theorem 10(ii) argument** (ledger row 50): L7 ("every spindle rotation at distance `d` puts `1/d²` into `Σ₀`, so no wall-broken plane has an additive `k`-colouring for `k ≤ 10`") and his proof of Theorem 10(ii) are the same argument — a norm-one `ξ` has `ξ + ξ̄ ∈ Σ₀`, hence `1/k ∈ Σ₀`, and an additive `k`-colouring `v` to a group of order `k` would need `k·v(1/k) = v(1) ≠ 0` while `k·G = 0`; a spindle rotation at distance `d` is exactly a norm-one element with `ρ + ρ̄ = 2 − 1/d²`. His Example 4 (`N = 167`) already makes G38's point in 1990: *"even though the plane Q(√167)² can be 7-colored since R² can, it has no additive 7-coloring."* What stays ours in L7: the spindle-rotation instantiation and the tie to the 2-adic wall. **§1 poses the question FS-36 answers for one class of fields**: *"This still begs the general question, however, whether some algebraic criterion can be found to determine the chromatic number of F² where F is a subfield of R, of finite degree over Q."* Theorem 8 is also the converse half of FS-36's multiquadratic two-colour criterion: an odd `e ≡ 3 (mod 4)` in the square-class group puts `ℚ(√e)`'s odd cycle into `F²`. Also his: **Theorem 7, a polynomial test for 2-colourability** (integer `p` with a root `ξ ∈ ℚ(i)`, `‖ξ‖ = 1` ⟹ `Σaₖ ≡ 0 mod 2`) — sharper than anything we built, and the mirror of `[CervantesKrebs2023]`'s Example 2.14. **Re-read 2026-09-19 at FS-61's prior-art gate (statements, in the text layer), three attributions this project had wrong or missing:** (a) **the exact value 3 is his** — Thm 8's "only if" half is the lower bound `χ ≥ 3` for every `N ≡ 3 (mod 4)`, and the sentence after Thm 9 reads *"the chromatic number of the plane Q(√N)² is 3 when N ≡ 3 or 7 mod 12"* (so `χ(ℚ(√3)²) = χ(ℚ(√7)²) = 3`, twenty years before `[Moorhouse2010]` Thm 8.6 and `[Madore2015]` Prop. 4.3, which FS-58's record credits); (b) **Thm 10 splits FS-61's census family at exactly its own modulus** — an additive 4-colouring if `N ≡ 3 (mod 8)`, no additive colouring with few colours if `N ≡ −1 (mod 24)`; (c) **Thm 6 is `CLAUDE.md` G63** — `F^d` is k-colourable iff `F(α)^d` is, for real `α` transcendental over `F`, proved by evaluating at 0 — and his Remark 1 (every finitely generated subfield of ℝ is purely transcendental over a number field) is the false step G63 names as the remaining gap. The 1994 sequel is `[Fischer1994]`. **Re-read 2026-09-23 (FS-71 batch 5c, prior-art gate on GPT Astra's Theorem 2.1):** Theorem 1 ("2-colourable ⟺ ring homomorphism Σ₀ → ℤ/2") is his general-`F` criterion, not restricted to the quadratic case Theorems 8–10 treat — GPT Astra's `χ(F²) = 2 ⟺ L(F) = 2` (every real number field, `ASTRA-2026-09-14-T3`) rests on this theorem's mechanism, translating the abstract ring-homomorphism existence into the checkable arithmetic form "a conjugation-stable dyadic prime with trivial residue involution" via lying-over — the translation is new, the mechanism is Fischer's **Cited by `[Johnson2006]` as its [7]** (read 2026-09-27), whose p. 213 restates the additive k-colouring for every `G(S, D)` with `S` an additive subgroup of ℝⁿ. **Theorem 1(i) is Theorem A's coset construction** (re-read in the source 2026-09-27, job LP): p. 181 defines an additive k-colouring as *"A group homomorphism v: C₀(F^d) → G to an additive group G of order k so that v(ξ) ≠ 0 whenever ‖ξ‖ = 1"*, `C₀(F^d)` the subgroup the unit vectors generate, and Thm 1(i) (p. 183, *"If F^d has an additive k-coloring, then F^d can be k-colored"*) is proved by copying the colouring from `C₀` to its translates — the homomorphism out of a subgroup, extended coset by coset, that `[Madore2015]` Prop. 3.9 declined to formulate; `LEDGER.md` row 57 and `CLOSED-QUESTIONS.md` §1.2 now say so. His Thms 8, 9, 10(i) give the values and upper bounds of the quadratic table in the kernel since FS131-LJ1 (`ThreeFourColours.chromaticNumber_planeOver_sqrt_table`). His Thm 1(i)–(iii) are three kernel statements (`DyadicQuadratic.colorable_planeOver_of_addMonoidHom`, `TorsionExtraction.colorable_two_iff_fieldBandSet_two_nonempty`, `ThreeColourCriterion.exists_maximal_conj_trivial_of_colorable_two` with `fieldBandSet_two_nonempty_of_conj_trivial`). | `library/papers/1-s2.0-0012365X9090324B-main.pdf` | **read in full (2026-09-08, FS-18); re-read 2026-09-24 by FS-81's planner (§1, Thms 8–10, Ex. 4, the ending) and verifier (Thm 9, against §F.5) — FS-81's relation `1 = n(2 − v − v̄)` is his Thm 10(ii) and Ex. 4, which conclude only the additive case (`LEDGER.md` row 82) re-read 2026-10-13 by FS131-KJ1 (Thm 1 and its proof, Thms 7–10 and their proofs, Ex. 4, against the kernel statements of the three-and-four-colours line): Thm 1(i)–(iii) are three kernel statements; the two-colour and three-colour maps are his; his four-colouring at `N ≡ 3 (mod 8)` is `ℤ/(4)`-valued and is not the kernel's.** |

| **[Chilakamarri1988]** | K. B. Chilakamarri, *Unit-distance graphs in rational n-spaces*, Discrete Math. 69 (1988) 213–218, doi 10.1016/0012-365X(88)90049-0 | Connectivity and **clique numbers** of `Uₙ = G(ℚⁿ)`. Theorem 2: `U₁…U₄` disconnected; Theorem 3: `Uₙ` connected for `n ≥ 5`. **Theorems 6–9 determine `ω(n)` completely**: for even `n`, `ω(n) = n+1` if `n+1` is a perfect square and `n` otherwise; for odd `n`, if `nx² − 2(n−1)y² = z²` has a solution with `x ≠ 0` then `ω(n) = n+1` or `n` according as `(n+1)/2` is or is not a perfect square, else `ω(n) = n−1`. His Lemma 1 (*"b is either odd or twice an odd integer"*, via squares mod 8) is the **rational shadow of our unit-vector `𝔭`-integrality lemma** — but it is used only to disconnect `U₄`, never to build a colouring. No fields, no residue map, no open problems. Cites Woodall only Cited by `[Johnson2006]` as its [3], p. 210, as a reproof of Benda–Perles's χ(ℚ³) = 2 and χ(ℚ⁴) = 4 (with Johnson's [12]). Cited by `[Johnson2000]` as its [3] (p. 177: `(ℚⁿ, 1)` connected iff `n ≥ 5`, *"as shown in COMS and, independently, by Chilakamarri [3]"*). | `library/papers/1-s2.0-0012365X88900490-main.pdf` | **read in full (2026-09-08, FS-18)** |

Chow, *Distances forbidden by two-colorings of ℚ³ and Aₙ*, Discrete Math. 115 (1993) 95–102, doi 10.1016/0012-365X(93)90481-8 | **The sharpest of the rational-base-case papers, and it carries our 2-adic invariant explicitly.** His **two-index** of a distance — the `k` in `d = √(2^k p/q)`, `p, q` odd — is exactly `v₂(d²)`, and **Theorem 1 is a COMPLETE classification**: a distance set `D` is forbidden by some two-colouring of `X ∈ {ℚ³, A₁, A₂, …}` iff every `X`-attainable element of `D` has the same two-index `k`, with `k` even for `ℚ³`, `k = 0` for `Aₙ (n ≥ 4)`, `k` even and ≥ 0 for `A₂, A₃`, `k ≥ 0` for `A₁`; necessary AND sufficient. Theorems 3–4: `d` is a legal scale factor for `ℚ³`/`A₃` iff `d = r√n` with `n ≡ 1 (mod 8)`. Method is still odd cycles; all colourings imported from `[Johnson1990]`. **The open direction this hands us (FS-19): the analogue over a number field — classify the distance sets forbidden by the `N(𝔭)`-residue colouring by `v_𝔭(d²)` — appears unasked, and Theorem A already has the machinery.** Cites Johnson and Jungreis–Reid–Witte; **does NOT cite `[Fischer1990]`, which is in the SAME journal volume (Discrete Math. 82, 1990) on the same problem** | `library/papers/1-s2.0-0012365X93904818-main.pdf` | **read in full (2026-09-08, FS-18)** |

What it does give: the *range* `ran(G)` of an edge-bicoloured graph (the set of `d` for which it draws with red edges at 1 and blue at `d`) is always a finite union of intervals with algebraic endpoints (Prop. 10, Tarski–Seidenberg), and **every such set with positive finite bounds is some graph's range** (Thm 11) — so the `{1,d}` family's `d`-parameter has no hidden rigidity for the dial to exploit. Three facts worth holding: **Bukh's conjecture** (`[Bukh2008]`) that forbidding an additional *transcendental* distance leaves `χ(ℝ²)` unchanged — the two-distance analogue of this project's whole field programme, and the reason the interesting `d` are algebraic; **`[Maehara1991]`**, that a number is realizable as a distance in a *rigid* unit-distance graph iff it is algebraic (M59's rigidity theory, from the realizability side); and his Lemma 7, that reversing the edge colouring inverts the range (`d ↔ 1/d`) — the realizability face of the scaling that carries a `{1,d}`-graph to a `{1,1/d}`-graph. **One terminology collision**: his "partly virtual edge-bicoloured graph" uses *virtual* for a construction device (green edges constrained to equal vectors), unrelated to the Polymath sense in `[Heule2019]` (§ E) | `library/pdf/arXiv-2601.07828-agoston-two-distance-range.pdf` | **read (2026-09-09, FS-23)** |

Hungar. 25(3–4) (1974) 335–340, doi 10.1007/BF01886093, zbMATH 0294.05108 (received 14 May 1973) — uniquely colourable products | **Thm 1 (p. 335):** *"Suppose G is a connected graph such that any two n-colourings of G differ in at least two points. Thm 3: line- and point-critical extensions. **Thm 4:** every `n`-colouring of an infinite power of `K_n` (`n ≥ 3`) is induced by factor colourings and a 0–1 measure (Pósa's observation; Lempert independently). Question (b), p. 335, is Hedetniemi's conjecture, "as far as we know, unanswered" in 1974. **FS-67 holds for `G = K_k^s`** (Thm 1 iterated, PA-P3; among the tight residue targets `Γ₃ ≅ K₃ × K₃` and `U₂ = K₄` with their powers, PA-P4), and Case 2 of Thm 1's proof is the `k`-colouring of `E_k(K_k)` FS-67's restatement asks for (PA-P2). It does not carry FS-58 D3.4: that `S₁` (81 vertices, degree 8) is no direct power of `K₃`; the general-factor form is Duffus–Sands–Woodrow's conjectural B(n). Parts, *Tiling the plane with hexagons: improved separations for k-colourings*, arXiv:2206.12635; Geombinatorics 32(2) (2022) 57–71 | **Genuine prior art for the LANDSCAPE of M37–M43's cell family, studied from the dual direction**; reached through `[Parts2023]`'s reference list. They fix the tile diameter at 1 and maximise the separation `d(k)` between same-coloured tiles over lattice-sublattice colourings of congruent hexagons (regular, *semi-regular*, *rectilinear*), for `3 ≤ k ≤ 175`; we fix the cell scale and ask for the fewest colours. The two are inverse functions of one another and **they agree exactly wherever they overlap** — verified here at three points: with `c = √3/(2p)` the unit distance in their normalisation, a coset colouring works iff `d(k) > c`, so M40's band boundary `p = √(3/7) ≈ 0.6547` is exactly `c = d(7) = √7/2`, `p = 1/2` is exactly `c = d(9) = √3`, and on `p ∈ (0.4804, 1/2)` the least admissible index is 12 (`d(10)`, `d(11)` do not beat `d(9)`; `d(12) = 2`), which is M40's `χ = 12` band and its index-12 coset colouring. **What they have that was published before us**: `d(6) ≈ 0.992076 < 1` (rectilinear hexagons; improving `[Ivanov2007]`'s `0.991445` and a pentagonal `√(55/56)`), i.e. **no hexagonal lattice-sublattice 6-colouring separates the unit distance** — the floor of M37/M38, computed in 2022; the closed forms `d(k) = (√3/2)(a−1)` for `k = a²` and `√((3a+3b−4)² + 3(a−b)²)/4` for Löschian `k = a²+ab+b²` (`d(7) = √7/2`, `d(9) = √3`, `d(12) = 2` — all matching our bands); and **the non-monotonicity of `d(k)` in the number of colours, with their §4 "Redundant colours?" asking whether some colours are outright redundant** — G23's phenomenon, in the dual variable, three years earlier. **What survives as ours, and the boundary is sharp**: they colour only by sublattices, we allow arbitrary labels (M38) — and the gap is measurable, since on the G\* band `p ∈ (0.4330, 0.4804)` their best coset colouring needs **12** colours while an arbitrary cell-constant colouring needs **7** (M40/M42, kernel-certified through the 23-vertex core, with `no_coset_seven_on_gap` proving no index-7 coset colouring exists there) — so the whole `ω = 3, χ = 7` pathology of G24 lives precisely where their method cannot look. Also unaffected: our proofs (theirs is numerical optimisation in Mathematica, and they say the search "was in no way exhaustive"), the exact values above seven on named scale bands, and the finite kernel-checkable carriers. **One edge the literature has not drawn**: their §4 *"Towards d(6) = 1"* argues only that *"a block-structured 6-tiling of the plane excluding distance 1 can only use tiles with at most five edges"* and leaves the possibility open — **`[SokolovVoronov2025]`'s `χ_poly(ℝ²) = 7` closes it in the negative** | `library/pdf/arXiv-2206.12635.pdf`; the journal copy `library/geombinatorics/geombinatorics-v32-n2-2022-10.pdf` (XXXII(2) 57–71; § G) | **read (2026-09-09, FS-23)** |

Results: `e5 ≥ 99`, `v5 ≥ 28`, `e6 ≥ 182`, `v6 ≥ 42`, and `v7 ≥ 6456` by a pentagonal tiling — below their own earlier `v7 > 6992`. **Footnote 2 is the load-bearing sentence for us:** *"In [5] the corresponding 509-vertex graph has 2442 edges, but is not edge-critical, which allows us to reduce e5. We were able to discard 36 edges, but we didn't perform an exhaustive search, so further improvements are possible."* — so the published edge state at 509 vertices is **2,406**; M63 §S.1 #4's secondary bar of 2,442 was already beaten in print and was corrected on 2026-09-11; and an **exhaustive** edge minimisation at 509 vertices is unrun by anyone (M63 phase D6). Also fixes the room below the record: **nothing published obstructs any vertex count between 28 and 509**. Cites two works named here and not held: `[Pritikin1998]` (JCTB 73 — `v7 > 6197`, `v5 > 12`) and Gwyn–Stavrianos, *Geombinatorics* 32(1), 2022 — named here, not held, no key minted | `library/pdf/arXiv-2303.14714-degrey-parts-lower-bounds.pdf` | **read in full (2026-09-11) — four pages** |

Heule, *Odd-distance virtual edges in unit-distance graphs*, Geombinatorics 31(2) (October 2021) 68–76 (zbMATH 1481.05047) | **The direct prior art for ledger row 59 (the 2-adic law of four-colour compulsions)**, known through `[PartsOdd2022]` §4 — and, as Parts reports it, a statement whose proof is asked for in print. Heule states a duality: *"if an odd distance `d` is a `k`-virtual edge of a subset `S` of the Euclidean plane, then distance `1/d` is a `k`-virtual edge in `S` as well"*, and (per Parts's report of a private correspondence) means it not of a finite graph but of *"larger objects like `ℚ(√−3,√−11)`"* — the degree-4 field `ℚ(ζ₆, ω₃)` that the Moser lattice spans, whose points form a rank-4 subset of our `K²` (the field as printed in the held `[PartsOdd2022]`, checked 2026-09-10). Parts reports it *"partially confirmed by pairs of distances of the form `3^a` and `3^{−a}`, which are indeed 4-virtual edges in `ℚ(√−3,√−11)`"*, gives a finite counterexample at `d = 5`, and writes that **"doubts remain about the correctness of the statement. Therefore, a more accurate formulation of the statement and its proof are of certain interest."** Our law supplies exactly that, since it holds over all of `K²` and so on that field's points: a virtual edge at four colours over `K` requires `v_𝔭(d) = 0`, and `v_𝔭(1/d) = −v_𝔭(d)`, so **the necessary condition is invariant under `d ↦ 1/d` and the odd integers are precisely the integer case of `v_𝔭(d) = 0`** — which is why the duality had to be stated for odd distances and why the finite counterexample does not touch it (the condition is necessary, not sufficient). **`[Heule2021b]`, free online, states the same rule in Heule's own words** and serves as the citation meanwhile; this paper stays wanted for the exact duality statement before any write-up of row 59. There is no preprint, and no citing work reproduces the statement's exact form. **It also presents a 1,441-vertex Moser-spindle-free 5-chromatic unit-distance graph** — the zbMATH reviewer's review (Zbl 1481.05047, read through the API 2026-09-21): *"a 5-chromatic unit-distance graph of 1441 vertices without Moser Spindles is presented"*; `[Haugland2026]` p. 2 calls it a reduction of `[Voronov2021]`'s 64,513. **Its coordinate field is not in any record we hold** (FS-70's gate question; the owner's reading, unverified: a trim of Voronov's graph, hence ℚ(√2,√3,√5)). **Acquisition** (loan list item [5]; **priority raised 2026-09-21** — it is now a gate question for FS-70): the author (Carnegie Mellon), the journal's subscription digital archive, or an interlibrary loan **Its Moser-free 1,441-vertex graph, per `[Soifer2024]` ch. 54 (read 2026-09-21; the paper itself is not held): `T721` in ℚ(√2,√3)² with a distance-2 pair monochromatic in every 4-colouring, found "in consultations with Vsevolod Voronov", trimmed by the clausal-proof method, spindled to `T1441` (so in ℚ(√2,√3,√5)²); building block a ten-vertex four-chromatic graph Soifer calls the Heule spindle. Not a ℚ(√3,√5) object.** | not held (Geombinatorics) | **not read — the duality statement quoted here is `[PartsOdd2022]` §4's quotation of it; the 1,441-vertex graph is known only from the zbMATH review and Haugland's sentence** **T721, web search 2026-09-24 (FS-85 §PA):** MathWorld's "Heule Spindle" page; a search snippet attributes to `[Haugland2026]`'s HTML version a 718-vertex virtual-edge gadget obtained from T721 by deleting three vertices; the held v3 PDF contains no "T721" — unchecked |

Their subject is Schreier–Sims (SST) cuts in integer programming — they show separating the integer hull enriched by generic SST cuts can be NP-hard even for integral `P`, and identify **stringent SST cuts**, which keep separation polynomial, plus SST clique cuts giving a complete linear description; a computational study says the stringent family dominates. **Caveat recorded before anyone reads it as a solution:** their results are for perfect and bipartite graph classes, and their computational study reports nothing on vertex-transitive or Cayley graphs — our targets are neither perfect nor small-`|Aut|`, and **the clique cuts that carry their complete description are exactly what triangle-freeness denies us at `ω = 2` members** (G57). So this is a method to port, not a result to cite. Nothing of ours rests on it | not held | **abstract read (2026-09-18); the paper not read** |

Prop. 6's tri-sector colourings are the non-additive colourings of `R₁` that the additive screen cannot see and the Kempe screen's swaps generate. Geom. 30 (2003) 573–589; doi 10.1007/s00454-003-2873-1; arXiv:math/0203025 | **The exact formula for the quantity M63 phase H2 exists, and it is here** (`plans/M63-phaseH2-prior-art.md` §PA.0 finding 2). **Theorem 1**: for a planar module `O` that is the ring of integers of a class-number-one field, over the real subring `Ō`, the number of module points on the circle of squared radius `r² ∈ Ō` is `c(r²) = N·∏_{p \| r², p splits}(v_p(r²)+1)`, **zero unless every inert prime divides `r²` to an even power**, with `N` = the number of units of `O` on the unit circle. **Corollary 1** rewrites it as `N·Σ_{𝖆 \| r²Ō} χ(𝖆)` with `χ` the quadratic character of `O/Ō`. **Fact 1**: `c` depends on `r²` only through the ideal it generates. **The Remark after the proof is what makes it ours**, verbatim: *"It is clear that Theorem 1 can be generalised to the situation that K is a totally complex field extension of a totally real field k whenever K has class number one … the prefactor has to be replaced by the number of elements in the unit group of O that lie on the unit circle."* That is `k = ℚ(√3,√11)`, `K = k(i)`, `N = 12` exactly. **Verified here at six independent points on our own field, including the mission's own circle** (§PA.3): the formula is exact on the maximal order `O_L` and **false on the sub-order `O_k ⊕ i·O_k`**, where our measured counts break multiplicativity. | **[Schaal1965]** | W. Schaal, *On the expression of a number as the sum of two squares in totally real algebraic number fields*, Proc. Amer. Math. He defines `f(ξ,𝖆)` = the number of solutions of `ξ = μ² + ν²` with `μ, ν ∈ 𝖆`, for `𝖆` **an ideal of `K` which may be integral or fractional** — literally our count over the module `𝖆 ⊕ i𝖆` — and proves a **mean value**, not an exact count: `Σ_{ξ ∈ 𝖆², 0<ξ^(h)<x_h} f(ξ,𝖆) = (π^n/(d·N(𝖆)²))·x₁···x_n + O((x₁···x_n)^{n/(n+1)+δ})`. Dividing by the count of candidate radii gives **mean occupancy `π^n/√\|d_K\| = π⁴/132 ≈ 0.738`, independent of `𝖆`** — so refining a module does not raise the mean occupancy of a circle, it adds circles, and the record's 30 is a ~40× fluctuation rather than a typical value. His `f(ηξ,𝖆) = f(ξ,𝖆)` for `η` a square of a unit is the orbit structure in 1965 dress. Nagell, *On the number of representations of an A-number in an algebraic field*, Arkiv för Matematik 4 (1962) nr. 37, 467–478; doi 10.1007/BF02591627; Zbl 0107.04001 | **The finiteness of our count, completely classified, in 1962.** An *A-number* is an element representable as a sum of two integral squares. **Theorem 7**: there are infinitely many representations of every A-number in every algebraic field **except** `ℚ(i)` and the **totally real** fields. **Theorem 4** is our case: a totally real base gives finitely many representations, exactly one of `1`, of every A-prime and of every A-unit, and *"a unit is an A-number only when it is a square"* — proved by the archimedean bound, i.e. compactness of the norm-one torus in elementary dress. **Theorem 1** is the product owner's link 1 in elementary clothing: a nontrivial solution of `1 = ξ²+η²` generates infinitely many by taking powers, the sole obstruction being that the element is a root of unity. **§6 solves the relative norm-one equation completely for biquadratic CM fields** `ℚ(i,√−D)`, the criterion being whether `(2)` is the square of a principal ideal in `ℚ(√D)`. **Caution on using his numbers: his unit of count is an orbit of the order-8 group generated by multiplication by `i` and by conjugation**, and his squares are required in `O_K`, so his counts are neither our module counts nor `[BaakeGrimm2003]`'s. Fischer (George Mason University), *A planar geometric graph of chromatic number four*, Congressus Numerantium 104 (December 1994, Winnipeg) 73–79 (Zbl 0836.05030; ISSN 0384-9864; no DOI, no electronic edition, in no citation database — found 2026-09-19 by FS-61's prior-art sweep through the zbMATH API, the mechanism recorded in `WORKFLOW.md` 3.2 item 8; the "Twenty-fifth Southeastern Conference" attribution is a web listing's — the scan shows no conference name) | **Holds Theorem B's VALUE, with a complete proof, thirty-two years before this project's kernel proof.** Theorem: for squarefree coprime `p ≡ 3 (mod 16)`, `q ≡ 11 (mod 16)`, `pq ≡ 1 (mod 32)`, the unit-distance graph on `ℚ(√p,√q)²` has an *additive 4-colouring* — a homomorphism `v : Σ → ℤ/4` from the group the unit vectors generate, nonzero on every unit vector (**the whole plane, not a lattice**); with the Moser spindle (whose vertices `P = ((5√3−√11)/12, (5+√33)/12)`, `Q` he prints) and Golomb's graph in the field, *"the chromatic number of this graph is 4"* — in those words, the first exact value of a field plane past the Moser obstruction, answering Heiko Harborth's request (§1, personal communication) for *a planar geometric graph of chromatic number four*. The map: trace to `ℚ(√pq, i)`, then the coordinate sum, then reduction mod 4 — `v(ξ) = 2(a₀+b₀+a₃+b₃)/c` for `ξ = (α₀+α₁√p+α₂√q+α₃√pq)/c` in lowest terms, `α_k = a_k+b_k i`; six lemmas of 2-adic congruences (`c ≢ 0 mod 8`; the valuation of `c` against `S = a₀+b₀+a₃+b₃ mod 8`; `S ≢ 0 mod 8`). **Verified here (2026-09-22): every lemma by hand, no gap; `tools/fischer1994_check.py` checks the identities symbolically, the mod-16 step by brute force, and every lemma and the theorem on 181,160 unit vectors of `ℚ(√3,√11,i)` and on two more fields of his family, with `ℚ(√3,√5)` (wall broken) as the control that goes red.** `~/source/library/notes/fischer-1994-reading.md` has the reading. **Consequences:** `CLAUDE.md` § Publication's trigger did not fire — the value is his; `LEDGER.md` row 57, `CLOSED-QUESTIONS.md` §1.1 and FS-20 are re-scoped. **What stays ours:** the kernel proof; Theorem A's general form (his family is a proper subfamily of the wall-standing fields Theorem A four-colours — `p, q ≡ 3 (mod 8)` suffices, and the criterion is G35's); the `𝔽₄`-valued residue colouring, which is **not his colouring**: his is `ℤ/4`-valued, exponent 4 — `v(P) = 1`, so it separates the distance-2 pair `(0, 2P)` that every residue colouring identifies (G41); and every consumer of B. **Reading (tools):** his `ρ` is the 2-adic embedding `√pq ↦ 1` in disguise — with `√pq` read as its 2-adic root mod 16 the same functional colours every `ℚ(√p,√q)` with `p ≡ 3`, `q ≡ 1, 3 (mod 8)` sampled (the fields completing to `ℚ₂(ζ₁₂)` above 2), so the `ℤ/4` colouring is local at 2 at a finite tower level; his congruences are what let it be written with `u = 1`. Also his: the remark that the four-colouring *precludes a five-chromatic graph with coordinates in* `ℚ(√p,√q)`; an aside that whether `[Wormald1979]`'s graph lies in `ℚ(√3,√11)²` is unclear (unanswerable as posed — that drawing has no coordinate field). References: `[Fischer1990]`, `[Wormald1979]` — both already held and read; nothing new to mine **Rests on it (as a control):** rows 89 and 100 use its ℤ/4 colouring as a positive control by formula (one dyadic prime, level 3). **Cited — by `[Johnson2006]` as its [8]** (read 2026-09-27), whose p. 214 states the theorem and the value: *"In [8] Fischer proves that if p and q are square-free relatively prime positive integers satisfying p ≡ 3 mod 16, q ≡ 11 mod 16, and pq ≡ 1 mod 32, then G(ℚ²(√p, √q), 1) has an additive 4-coloring. Since G(ℚ²(√3, √11), 1) contains the Moser spindle, it follows that χ(G(ℚ²(√3, √11), 1)) = 4."* It is still in no citation database; `WORKFLOW.md` 3.2 item 8's "nothing cites it" is false (a draft for the owner, `data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lp/record.md`). Also cited by `[Johnson2000]` as its [9] (p. 176), with the value. | `library/papers/fischer-1994-planar-geometric-graph-chromatic-number-four-congr-numer-104-pp73-79.pdf` (the library scan, 300 dpi, cover sheet and contents page in front) | **read in full, every page, by de Bruijn (2026-09-22); every lemma verified by hand and the theorem by computation** |

The second sequel to `[Johnson1996fcn]`: Leader's and Levin's examples (Scheinerman–Ullman, *Fractional Graph Theory*, 1997, Exercises 23–24, as Johnson cites them) show `sup χ_f(H) < χ_f(G)` can happen; Levin's `G_∞` (a countable clique, each edge subdivided twice; `χ_f = 3`, finite parts `→ 1 + √2`) is an induced subgraph of `(ℝ², 1)`, so the de Bruijn–Erdős analogue fails for `χ_f` inside the plane; *"the restrictions of those questions to geometric graphs of interest here, such as the unit distance graph in the plane, are still open"* (p. 135). Its Problems (pp. 140–143), verbatim in the record: 1, a subgraph of `ℝ²_ℚ` (rational distances); **2, *"For which positive integers m ≡ 3 mod 4 is there an induced subgraph of (ℚ²(√m), 1) isomorphic to G_∞?"*** (p. 141; unanswered here); **3–5 (p. 143)**, *"Is it true that χ_f(ℝ², 1) = sup[χ_f(H); H is a finite subgraph of (ℝ², 1)]? Is χ_f(ℝ², 1) = χ_f(𝒜², 1) …? Is it true that for every ε > 0 there exists a non-bipartite finite subgraph H of (ℝ², 1) satisfying χ_f(H) < 2 + ε?"* — 3 and 4 answered given MR (row 129 and a derived sandwich, `CLOSED-QUESTIONS.md` §3), 5 elementary. Cites `[Fischer1990]` and `[Johnson1987]` (p. 141: *"(ℚ²(√m), 1) is bipartite for every square-free positive integer m equal to 1 or 2 mod 4"*). Verdict: **context, not load-bearing** — states that representability over number fields is a global problem in which **the class number is involved**, that Nagell's method depends on `h(ℚ(√d,√−d)) = 1` and "cannot be applied to general algebraic number fields", and uses the integral Brauer–Manin obstruction. Supports §PA.3's principality caveat |
| `pdf/rudnick-2018-lattice-points-on-circles-notes.pdf` | — | Z. Rudnick, *Lattice points on circles*, open-problems course notes, Tel Aviv 2018. Read: §1.1–§1.2 in full, §1.3–§1.5 skimmed. Verdict: **the classical extremal picture for `ℤ²` only** — the divisor bound, Jarník, Cilleruelo–Córdoba. No covolume statement anywhere |
| `pdf/cilleruelo-lattice-points-on-circles.pdf` | — | J. Cilleruelo, *Lattice points on circles*. Read: abstract, §1. Verdict: **about the angular distribution** of the `r(n)` points, not a count and not a covolume statement |

**Held files with no key — verdicts.** These came with the 2026-09-05 fetch and were scanned on 2026-09-08
(FS-18, `plans/STUB-library-read.md` §R) against five questions: a chromatic result over a field other than ℝ or ℚ;
a citation of Moorhouse / Fischer / Vinh / Medrano–Myers–Stark–Terras / Krebs / Benda–Perles / Johnson; stated open
problems; a residue/congruence colouring; anything else load-bearing. The papers from that fetch that did bear on
us have rows in § A (`[Manturov2012]`, `[CranstonRabern2015]`, `[CurrieEggleton2016]`, `[Payne2009]`,
`[JoeNoble2023]`); these are recorded as explicit negatives so they are not re-scanned.

| arXiv | title | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 2103.02757 | Zapletal, *Coloring the distance graphs in three dimensions* | **Negative, and superseded by 2201.00275 below.** `Δⁿ` is the *rational-distance* graph, not unit-distance; the content is choiceless set theory (consistency of "χ(Δⁿ) countable while χ(Δⁿ⁺¹) is not"). Real closed subfields of ℝ appear only as a forcing device. Cites none of the named authors. No bearing |
| 2201.00275 | Zapletal, *Coloring the distance graphs* | **Negative.** Supersedes 2103.02757 (its Theorem 1.1 proves that paper's Conjecture 1.1 for all `n`). If either is ever cited, cite this one. No field result, no residue colouring, cites none of the named authors |
| 2106.05333 | Noble, *Assorted musings on dimension-critical graphs* | **Negative for prior art, one useful datum.** No fields; `dim(G)` is the least `n` with a unit-distance representation in `ℝⁿ`. Records under his Question 5 that as of 2023 *"it is unknown as to whether there even exists `G` satisfying `dim(G) = 2` and `χ(G) ∈ {6,7}`"* — outside confirmation that our target is open. Also Erdős–Harary–Tutte's `dim(G) ≤ 2χ(G)` and Schaefer's NP-completeness of planar unit-distance representability |
| 2108.07713 | Noble, *Embedding Euclidean distance graphs in `ℝⁿ` and `ℚⁿ`* | **Negative.** `ℚⁿ` only, never a proper extension and never a finite field. Heavy three-squares / mod-8 congruence work, but for *realizability* of distances, never to build a colouring. Cites Johnson as a co-author only |
| 2305.07770 | Brouwer, Joe & Noble, *Odd vector cycles in `ℤᵐ`* | **Negative for prior art, one nice hook.** `ℤᵐ` and `ℚ³`; the 2-adic content is only the coordinate-sum parity split. Cites `[Manturov2012]` — the one citation by which the finite-plane reduction sat next to this community without anyone connecting it to number fields. The hook: they prove that their Conjecture 1 would re-derive Arno's class-number-4 theorem without its computer proof — a combinatorial statement about odd cycles implying a deep number-theoretic classification, the genre our programme aims at from the other side |
| — | Raigorodskii, Borsuk survey (Russian), `pdf/raigorodskii-borsuk-survey-RU.pdf` | **Not read (Russian, no OCR run).** Its §§3–4 are `χ(ℚᵈ)` with proofs. `[Payne2009]` records that this survey **miscites Chilakamarri's `χ(ℚ⁵) = 8` conjecture as a theorem**, so it is not a safe secondary source. Low priority: the `ℚᵈ` direction is covered directly by the papers in § A |

## C. Titles seen in searches — each with its verdict

*A title enters this section only with its verdict, written the same day; a title that bears on our work is
promoted into § A with a read or a scan (§ D rule 0c). Every row below carries a verdict (swept row by row on
2026-09-09, FS-23), so nothing here is a standing debt.*

**Promoted into § A** (each read or scanned there): arXiv:2206.12635 → `[deGreyParts2022]`, prior art for the whole
cell family (ledger rows 34–37); arXiv:2206.12632 → `[PartsOdd2022]`, which states the observed form of ledger row
59's 2-adic law, over the Moser lattice's field `ℚ(√−3,√−11)`, in June 2022; arXiv:2303.14722 → `[Parts2023]`; arXiv:2201.04499 →
`[ChybowskaSokol2022]`; arXiv:2206.12633 → `[Parts2022]`; doi 10.1007/s00454-004-1092-8 → `[Exoo2005]`;
arXiv:2311.10069 → `[MatolcsiEtAl2025]`, which names our direction ladder the **Moser lattice**; arXiv:2606.28157 →
`[DuczVarga2026]`, whose reference list led to `[Ducz2026]` and thence to `[Polymath16wiki]`; arXiv:2404.05509 →
`[Mundinger2024]`; arXiv:2502.01958 → `[SokolovVoronov2025]`; arXiv:2303.06262 and arXiv:2303.06272 →
`[CervantesKrebs2023]`; arXiv:1810.00960 → `[Bellitto2021]`; doi 10.1007/s00373-012-1249-9 → `[AxenovichEtAl2014]`; doi 10.1007/BF01858462 → `[Reid1996]`; Zbl 0558.05020 (L. A. Székely, 1984) → `[Szekely1984]` (FS-103's close).

**Swept and closed — each with the reason it cannot touch our questions:**

| item | verdict |
|---|---|
| arXiv:2502.17678 Burrin–Gröbner, small-scale equidistribution of rational points on S² (held); arXiv:1308.5173 Harangi–Virág, independence ratio from random eigenvectors (held) | **Tangential** (FS-58 §PA). The first shows what small-scale equidistribution statements look like, on the sphere over ℚ; the second gives *lower* bounds on independence ratios from λ_min, not an equality case |
| Chilakamarri, *A 4-chromatic unit-distance graph with no triangles*, Geombinatorics IV(3) (1995) 64–76, and *Some problems arising from unit-distance graphs*, IV(3) (1995) 104; Johnson, *Problems posed in or arising from "Colorings of Metric Spaces": status report*, IX(4) (2000) 170; O'Donnell, *Arbitrary girth 4-chromatic unit distance graphs*, IX(3)/IX(4) (2000); Hochberg–O'Donnell, V(4) (1996) 137–141; Jungić, *Still spinning*, Math. Mag. 96(2) (2023), doi 10.1080/0025570X.2023.2176684, Zbl 1535.05104 | **Owed, titles only** (FS-58 §PA; paywalled or behind the Geombinatorics archive's browser challenge). Johnson's 2000 status report is the likeliest printed form of a quadratic-field question; Chilakamarri's triangle-free graph has explicit coordinates (per `[Soifer2024]`), field unstated. The sweep's route list: `geombinatorics.uccs.edu/archive`, tandfonline for Jungić |
| Peyre, Duke 79 (1995); Batyrev–Tschinkel, IMRN 1995 no. 12 (anisotropic tori) and J. Alg. Geom. 7 (1998); Chambert-Loir–Tschinkel, arXiv:math/0005015; Petersen–Sinclair, IJNT 7 (2011) | **Background for route R1, not opened** (FS-58 §PA). Height zeta functions of tori use multiplicative Hecke characters; the vector-group case uses additive characters but not the circle; none gives additive-character sums over torus points uniformly. The sweep found no such uniform statement anywhere |
| Chang–Liu–Zhu, JCTB 75 (1999); Ngoc–Tuza, Discrete Math. 138 (1995); Exoo–Goedgebeur, arXiv:1805.06713; Sokolov, Math. Notes 103 (2018); Carrillo–Cervantes–Krebs–León, College Math. J. 56 (2025); Kang–Maharaj, Integers 10 (2010) | **Not opened; verdicts from listings** (FS-58 §PA): χ_f of integer distance graphs (1-D); 4-chromatic graphs of large odd girth (abstract graphs); girth, not odd girth; the affine chromatic number of ℚⁿ; Cayley graphs of ℤⁿ modulo a cyclic subgroup; p-adic distance graphs on ℤ. None bears on a field plane |
| arXiv:1712.00741 *Composition of Binary Quadratic Forms over Number Fields* | **Background only, not opened** (M63 phase H2, `plans/M63-phaseH2-prior-art.md` §PA.11). Gauss composition over a base ring — the machinery *behind* the ideal ↔ representation correspondence that `[BaakeGrimm2003]` Corollary 1 already states for us, so it adds nothing to the count. **Reopen if** the class-group correction for the sub-order failure (§PA.3 link 3) ever needs writing explicitly rather than measuring |
| arXiv:2112.14489 *Sums of squares of integer-multiple of an integral element on real bi-quadratic fields*; arXiv:2105.08860 *Pythagoras numbers of orders in biquadratic fields* | **Marginal, not opened** (M63 phase H2 §PA.11). **The only works found on our own field type** — a real biquadratic base — but about *representability* and Pythagoras numbers, not about counting representations. **Reopen if** the existence question over a specific sub-order arises; `α`'s representability is already settled two ways in §PA.3 |
| arXiv:2103.05322 *Sums of integral squares in complex bi-quadratic fields and in CM fields* | **Negative** (M63 phase H2 §PA.11). Grepped by the phase's norm-form reader: **does not mention Nagell or counting at all**; Pythagoras numbers only |
| arXiv:1802.06460 *Embedding distance graphs in finite field vector spaces* (Iosevich–Parshall; held) | **Negative.** Erdős–Falconer *distance-set* problems: large subsets of `𝔽_q^d` determine prescribed configurations, `d ≥ 2t`. No chromatic number, no number fields, no residue colouring. The nearest-title trap for M58 and it is not it |
| arXiv:2205.12312 *The chromatic number of `ℝⁿ` with multiple forbidden distances* (Naslund; held) | **Negative for the plane.** Asymptotics in `n` for `χ(ℝⁿ; m)` by the partition-rank method; `n = 2` appears only as background. Does not bear on `μ(d)`, which counts `d`-edges rather than distances |
| arXiv:2605.28709 D. Czifra, Á. Dúcz, M. Matolcsi, D. Varga, P. Zsámboki, *Improved bounds for the double cap conjecture*; arXiv:2607.05083 Á. Dúcz, *On the maximal measure of a spherical set avoiding solutions to x + y + z = 0* | **Titles only, not read** (FS105-W §4.3, the poser's zbMATH listing, 2026-09-26): sphere problems by their titles |
| arXiv:1810.00960 *On the density of sets … avoiding distance 1*; arXiv:2207.13892 (random `ε`-distance graphs on spheres) | **Background only.** The density side `m₁(ℝ²)` that `[MatolcsiEtAl2025]`'s Hall-ratio result now dominates; the first is `[Bellitto2021]`. The sphere paper is a random-graph model |
| arXiv:1010.3908 E. P. Bugarin, M. L. A. De Las Peñas, *Bravais colorings of N-fold tilings* (the arXiv title; OpenAlex lists it as *On Bravais colorings associated with periodic and non-periodic crystals*) | **Negative, and the debt is discharged** (M63 phase H3 gate, item 4). It was the single arXiv full-text hit for "planar module non-cyclotomic" and went unopened; **opened and grepped 2026-09-12**. Its abstract scopes it exactly: Bravais colourings of *"planar modules that emerge as the ring of integers in cyclotomic fields with class number one"*, the modules `M_n = ℤ[exp(2πi/n)]`, worked at `M₁₅` and `M₁₆`. **Cyclotomic only, and it does not touch our object**: with positive controls in the same file by the same instrument (`cyclotomic` 6, `class number one` 3), the counts for `non-cyclotomic`, `rank`, `CM field`, `totally real` and `submodule` are all **0** after undoing hyphenation and line breaks. The full-text hit was a false positive on "planar module" + "non-periodic" + "cyclotomic" as separate terms. **Read: abstract and introduction opening; whole text grepped with controls.** Not held |
| arXiv:1402.5013 P. Zeiner, *Similar submodules and coincidence site modules* | **Neighbourhood, not opened, and correctly so** (M63 phase H3 gate §PA.4). Its subject is **equal-rank** submodules — `[BaakeZeiner2017]` §6 defines a submodule of a planar module to be of the same rank, footnote 8 disclaiming the general notion — whereas phase H3's submodule is **rank-halving**, so the theory does not reach it. **Reopen only if an equal-rank submodule question ever arises here** | 
| В. Г. Журавлев (V. G. Zhuravlev), *Sums of squares over the Fibonacci ○-ring*, J. Math. Sciences (2007), doi 10.1007/s10958-007-0195-1 (translated from Zapiski POMI) **[title from a database listing; NOT read]** | **Found by M63 phase H3's two-database citation cross-check, and by nothing else** — neither phase H2's gate nor either of phase H3's sweeps surfaced it. It cites `[BaakeGrimm2003]` and its title is a **sums-of-two-squares count over a real quadratic ring** (the Fibonacci ring, i.e. the integers of `ℚ(√5)`), which is phase H2's front-2 question over a different base. **If it is what the title says, it is a worked non-cyclotomic case of exactly our count** — which `[BaakeZeiner2017]` Remark 7.12's four-item inventory does not list, so either the survey missed it or it is a different quantity. **Unresolved; by G50 it stays flagged and uncharacterised.** Russian-language original; a translation exists behind Springer | 
| arXiv:1908.03880 *Lattice approach to plane colorings* (S. Heinäsmäki) | **Negative, and a near-perfect false positive** (M63 phase H3 gate, `plans/M63-phaseH3-prior-art.md` §PA.3). The title reads as exactly our object; the paper is a **condensed-matter spin model** (`cond-mat.stat-mech`) approximating plane colourings by minimum-energy configurations of a fixed-range multicomponent spin system, concluding that the continuum limit suggests seven colours. No number field, no module, no unit-distance graph. **Read: abstract and subject class.** Recorded so the title is not re-chased |
| arXiv:2509.04591 *Constructions of lattices via biquadratic and triquadratic fields*; arXiv:1609.03134 *Construction of Arakelov-modular lattices from number fields* | **Negative, and the reason is the part that carries** (M63 phase H3 gate §PA.3). "Lattices from biquadratic fields" is a large active literature — algebraic lattices, ideal lattices, Arakelov-modular lattices — and it is about the **canonical embedding** `K → ℝ^{r₁} × ℂ^{r₂}`, which turns a degree-`n` field into a **discrete rank-`n` lattice in `ℝⁿ`. **Ours is a single complex place**, `K → ℂ`, giving a **dense** rank-`n` module in `ℝ²`. Different objects; nothing transfers. The room for a dense planar module is aperiodic order, which is where `[BaakeGrimm2003]` was found. **Read: abstracts and construction descriptions, deliberately no further** |
| arXiv:2303.06801 *A 5-chromatic same-distance graph in the hyperbolic plane* | **Negative.** Hyperbolic; no field structure, no transfer |
| arXiv:1305.2765 *Coloring distance graphs: a few answers and many questions* | **Scanned.** A survey of distance graphs on *non-Euclidean* spaces; its own framing is that only a few such spaces have been studied. No number fields |
| arXiv:1507.00025 *Chromatic number of Euclidean plane* | **Junk.** Its argument is that `χ(ℝ²) > 4` contradicts the four-colour theorem for planar graphs. Recorded so it is never fetched again |
| arXiv:2506.19151, arXiv:2506.19126 | **Negative.** Re-postings of 2000/2001 surveys on *upper* chromatic numbers (a different parameter) |
| arXiv:2604.15466 *Any 2-coloring … monochromatic unit rhombuses* | **Negative, one datum.** A compulsion theorem for the plane at **two** colours (a mono rhombus with unit sides and non-unit diagonals). Right genre, wrong rung |
| arXiv:2510.15002 (`ℤ²` unit-distance recognition NP-complete); arXiv:2312.15336 (Gray graph); arXiv:0711.1157, 0912.5395 (Heawood embeddings) | **Negative.** Realizability, not colouring; `[Schaefer2013]` is the general statement |
| arXiv:1111.1015, arXiv:1003.2588 (Banakh; Banakh–Chervak; both held) | **Negative.** Ramsey-theoretic *symmetry* numbers and centerpole sets of abelian groups — the name collision "colourings of abelian groups" is with `[CervantesKrebs2023]`, and these are unrelated |
| arXiv:1903.09444 (perfect 2-colourings of circulants with odd distances); arXiv:2508.19775 (Johnson-type graphs) | **Negative.** Perfect (equitable) colourings and Johnson graphs; neither is a plane or a field |

Not prior art for that identification |
| arXiv:2503.01721 (Lorenz–Zimmermann, *Diameter and Girth of Representation Graphs of Quadratic Forms*; held) | **Opened by package P2 and confirmed to stay on the FULL vector space, never the quotient** — so it does not touch P2's circulant. It is `[LorenzZimmermann2023]`'s companion, listed here so a gate that meets the number alone does not re-fetch it |
| arXiv:1908.03880 *Lattice approach to plane colorings* (Heinäsmäki; held) | **Negative, and a naming trap: "lattice" here is a discretisation grid, not an algebraic lattice.** A spin-model heuristic whose ground states approximate plane colourings; its conclusion (seven colours needed) is numerical evidence, not a theorem |
| doi 10.1007/978-0-8176-8092-3_8 (Soifer essay); doi 10.1007/978-3-540-28629-5_19 (group colouring complexity) | **Superseded or background.** `[Soifer2024]` covers the first; the second is a complexity question with no field content |
| *Kummer theory for multiquadratic or quartic cyclic number fields* (Perissinotto, Perucca; Unif. Distrib. Theory 17(2); `orbilu.uni.lu`) | **Negative by its listing, not opened.** Degrees of cyclotomic–Kummer extensions of multiquadratic fields; no square-class decomposition and no colouring. Seen in FS-36's prior-art search under the Kummer-theory vocabulary |
| arXiv:math/0511147 *Planar coincidences for N-fold symmetry* and arXiv:math/0511306 *Multiple planar coincidences with N-fold symmetry* (M. Baake, U. Grimm) **[titles from a search listing]** | **Negative, and informative: the discipline next door has solved the complement of our problem.** The crystallographic *coincidence site lattice* literature classifies every rotation carrying part of a planar lattice or module onto itself — all N < 46 — by ideal factorisation in cyclotomic fields; for the triangular lattice ℤ[ζ₃] the coincidence rotations are `ε·∏_{p≡1(3)}(ω_p/ω̄_p)^{t_p}` with index `Σ = ∏ p^{|t_p|}`, the group being `C₆ × ℤ^(ℵ₀)`. **Every such rotation has `ω/ω̄ = ω²/N(ω)`, hence cos ∈ ℚ and sin ∈ √3·ℚ, hence lies in `K(i)` — exactly the class Theorem B four-colours** and which M63 phase G rejects as its first filter (`plans/M63-phaseG-bases-and-rotations.md` §PA3, §2). The rotations this project needs are precisely the **non**-coincidence ones (Σ infinite), so the Σ-counting machinery does not transfer. Kept because it is the vocabulary in which *"which rotations relate two copies of this lattice"* is a named, classified question, and because it states in one line why maximal coupling is anti-correlated with usefulness (M62 §M2.1). Found by phase G's prior-art gate, 2026-09-11; **not read beyond the classification as the search listings state it** |

**Seen in the M63 phase H4 reducibility search (2026-09-12), each with its verdict — none read, all named from
secondary statements:** Appel–Haken (1977), *Every planar map is four colorable*, Illinois J. Math. 21 — the origin
of the unavoidable-set-of-reducible-configurations method; ring size ≤ 14, 1,936 configurations. Cited here only for
that ceiling, which `[Steinberger2010]` states. Robertson–Sanders–Seymour–Thomas, *A new proof of the four-colour
theorem*, Electron. Res. Announc. AMS 2 (1996) 17–25 — 633 configurations, ring size ≤ 14; the AMS PDF refused a
scripted fetch (403), so the ring bound is taken from `[Steinberger2010]`. Borodin (1977) and Erdős–Rubin–Taylor
(1979), independently: **a connected graph is degree-choosable iff it is not a Gallai tree** (every block a complete
graph or an odd cycle) — **the exact boundary of degree-based free deletion at four colours, and the sharpening of
our 4-core peel that phase H4 priced**. Cranston–Rabern, *Brooks' theorem and beyond*, arXiv:1403.0479, and *Beyond
degree choosability*, arXiv:1511.00350 — the modern survey and the extension past Gallai trees; the same two authors
as `[CranstonRabern2015]`, different works. **Verdict on all of the above: correctly identified as the classical
theory behind `Absorb.lean`, and retired for `ρ₆₃` by the pricing in `plans/M63-phaseH4-freecensus.md` §10 rather
than by anything in the papers.** Also seen and not relevant: arXiv:2412.18558 (configuration reducibility via gauge
theory — planar throughout); arXiv:1809.02807 (*The Birkhoff Diamond as Double Agent*); MDPI Mathematics 6 (2018)
309 (*Kempe-Locking Configurations*).

Not relevant (name collisions, recorded so they are not re-fetched): arXiv:2106.05621 (rationalizability of
field extensions, Feynman integrals); doi 10.1007/s00209-025-03728-4 (an algebraic Fischer–Grauert theorem);
arXiv:1301.3998 (dihedral group actions).
