| **[BrewsterEtAl2020]** *Graph homomorphism reconfiguration and frozen H-colourings* (R. C. Brewster, J.-B. Lee, B. Moore, J. A. Noel, M. Siggers; arXiv:1712.00200; J. Graph Theory 2020 per the search listing) | **Abstract read (2026-09-13).** Defines a *frozen* H-colouring as one that reconfigures to no other (a global property of one colouring); the k-colouring case ("every vertex sees all k−1 other colours") is the reconfiguration literature's standard notion. FS-38's *fan gap at v* is "v is not frozen in C"; the paper does not transport frozenness along a homomorphism G → H, which is FS-38's use. Vocabulary source for the FS-38 write-up (§ E: our "fan gap" is their "non-frozen vertex") |
| **[KlotzSander2017]** *Uniquely colorable Cayley graphs* (W. Klotz, T. Sander; Ars Math. Contemp. 12(1) (2017), doi 10.26493/1855-3974.879.d47) | **Abstract read (2026-09-13).** In a uniquely k-colourable Cayley graph the colour classes are cosets of a subgroup of index k. The opposite pole of FS-38's residue planes, which have *no* forced pair at all; cited for the vocabulary (unique colourability = every pair in a class forced, every pair across classes separated). Their Problem 3.6 not seen (full text not fetched) **Re-read for FS-85 (2026-09-24), summary only:** its Problem 3.6 (must a uniquely colourable circulant have χ = ω?) is FS-85 step 2's finite-abelian form; answered no at three colours by `[Basic2026]`. **Read 2026-09-24 (FS-85 input auditor): §§1, 3, 4 in full.** Thm 3.3: `Cay(ℤ_n, ±{1,…,k−1})` is uniquely k-colourable, χ = ω = k (edge-minimal). Problem 3.6 (p. 161) as quoted. **§4, Thm 4.2 (extending `[GreenwellLovasz1974]`): X uniquely n-colourable and 2K₂-free, Y connected with χ(Y) > n ⟹ X × Y (direct product) uniquely n-colourable; §4 notes the direct product of Cayley graphs is a Cayley graph over the product group.** With X = K_k and Y a connected triangle-free circulant of order prime to k with χ(Y) > k this gives a triangle-free uniquely k-colourable circulant for every k — a corollary the paper, which poses 3.6 a page earlier, does not draw (`plans/FS85-input-audit.md` A3.3). Citing works (OpenAlex W2562747661 and Semantic Scholar CorpusId 55418791, four each, not the same four; union five): `[JanssenMacKeigan2020]`, `[Hujdurovic2020]`, `[ErskineGriggsSiran2022]`, `[Matsumoto2021]`, `[Basic2026]` |
| **[Haemers2021]** *Hoffman's ratio bound* (W. H. Haemers; arXiv:2102.05529, v2 11 Sep 2021; Linear Algebra Appl. per the ScienceDirect listing S0024379521000689) | **§3 "History" and the equality passage read from the PDF (2026-09-13, the close's prior-art query for phase D's mechanism).** The ratio bound is Hoffman's but **unpublished**: told to him by Seidel from `[Delsarte1973]`'s strongly-regular clique bound (formula (3.22)) at a conference c. 1974; Lovász's Theorem 9 (`[Lovasz1979]`) implies it and over-credits Hoffman; the 1970 paper `[Hoffman1970]` is *"the wrong paper"* to cite for it. Equality: *"each vertex outside C has exactly … −λ_n neighbors in C"* (interlacing). No Cayley graphs, no characters, no colourings whose every class is tight. Held: `library/pdf/arXiv-2102.05529-haemers-ratio-bound.pdf`. References not yet mined (Godsil–Newman on the Laplacian form is the one in our neighbourhood) **FS-67 (J40, 2026-09-25): p. 4's equality passage is Lemma T (FS67-T, row 115)** — Hoffman-tight at `k` ⟹ every `k`-colouring gap-free. |
| **[AbiadEtAl2025]** *Hoffman colorability of (strongly) regular graphs* (A. Abiad, B. De Bruyn, T. van Veluw; arXiv:2508.18793, 26 Aug 2025) — and, named there as the source of the notion, A. Abiad, W. Bosma, T. van Veluw, *Hoffman colorings of graphs*, Linear Algebra Appl. 710 (2025) 129–150 (not held) | **§1–§2 read from the PDF (2026-09-13).** *"A graph for which the Hoffman bound is tight is called Hoffman colorable, and every optimal coloring of such a graph is called a Hoffman coloring"* — the literature's name for FS-38 phase D's "k-tight" (`plans/FS38-derivation.md` D-2 (a)). **Proposition 2.1, quoted from Blokhuis–Brouwer–Haemers 2007 (Des. Codes Cryptogr. 44, Prop. 2.3): *"If G is regular, then every color class of a Hoffman coloring is a Hoffman coclique. In other words, the color classes have equal size and every vertex is adjacent to exactly −λ_min(G) vertices of every color other than its own"*** — which is Theorem D2-A (i) and (iv) at once (a vertex adjacent to every other colour has no fan gap). Not in it, and not found elsewhere: the Fourier form on an abelian Cayley graph (every Hoffman colouring periodic under the common kernel of the minimising characters, D2-A (ii)–(iii)) and tightness propagating up a tower of quotients (Lemmas 1–3). Its own subject — strongly regular graphs, spreads, pseudo-geometricity — is not ours. Held: `library/pdf/arXiv-2508.18793-abiad-hoffman-colorability.pdf`. References named and not mined: Brouwer–Haemers *Spectra of graphs* (2012) Thm 3.5.2, Haemers–Tonchev on spreads **§4 Thm 4.1 read (J40, 2026-09-25):** a non-trivially Hoffman-colourable connected 2-walk-regular graph is not uniquely vector colourable — the uniqueness clause of `[GodsilEtAl2020]` Thm 4.16 (2); FS67-DC1 uses item (1), no conflict. |
| **[Basic2026]** *On uniquely colorable Cayley graphs* (M. Bašić; arXiv:2609.03184, 2 Sep 2026) | **Listing + summary (2026-09-13).** Answers two problems of `[KlotzSander2017]` (uniquely 3-colourable integral circulants with clique number 2; nonabelian uniquely colourable Cayley graphs whose classes are cosets of distinct subgroups). Not read; nothing of ours rests on it **FS-85 (2026-09-24): now bears** — a triangle-free (ω = 2) uniquely 3-colourable circulant family is triangle-free rigidity at three colours on finite abelian Cayley graphs, the finite form of FS-85 step 2 one colour down; finite quotients bear on `P`, not `F`. Seen at FS-38 with "nothing rests on it" and not connected when FS-85 was minted (a G50 instance). Still not read in full **Read 2026-09-24 (FS-85 planner; library row in § B): the mechanism.** `G_p = ICG_{12p}({2, p})`, the Cayley graph of ℤ/12p on the residues x with gcd(x, 12p) ∈ {2, p}, p ≥ 5 prime: triangle-free (ω = 2, by Bašić–Ilić 2009 Thm 3.1, not read), properly coloured by x mod 3 (an additive colouring), and uniquely 3-colourable because α = 4p = n/3 and the only independent sets of that size are the three residue classes mod 3 (Lemma 3.3, a combinatorial layer count on ℤ/4 × ℤ/p: two classes induce `K₂ ⊗ (C₄ □ K_p)`, Lemma 3.2). The Hoffman bound is not tight there (6(p−1) against 4p): the rigidity is an extremal-independent-set argument, not a spectral one. A finite (periodic) object: it bears on `P`, not `F`, for a lift to ℤ^r **§§4–5 read 2026-09-24 (FS-85 input auditor):** §4 answers Klotz–Sander's Problem 2.4 with `Cay(Γ, Γ∖U)`, U non-normal — a complete multipartite graph, no bearing on triangle-free colourings; §5 poses no every-k question. So Bašić stops at k = 3. Citing works: none in OpenAlex (W7208766586) or Semantic Scholar (2026-09-24) |

Its pivot property is the named tool for Coxeter's unproved reading that a winding class is one irreducible component **Row 119** (FS-75): its heights are one ingredient of the winding form, "not found stated". |
| **[ChandgotiaPakTassy2019]** N. Chandgotia, I. Pak, M. Tassy, *Kirszbraun-type theorems for graphs* (2019) **[title from the FS-73 P3 gate's listing; venue unchecked]** | **Listing only.** The natural prior art for the McShane-type extension step of FS-73's density theorem (H-iii, `plans/FS73-derivation.md` T24); **unread** — owed before any write-up claims the step |
| **[MooreNewman2000]** C. Moore, M. E. J. Newman, *Height representation, critical exponents, and ergodicity in the four-state triangular Potts antiferromagnet*, J. Stat. Phys. (2000); arXiv:cond-mat/9902295; zbMATH 0959.82005 (summary withheld) | **Read: abstract, §I–§III (FS-79 gate, 2026-09-23); held.** Proper four-colourings of the **triangular lattice** (the zero-temperature 4-state Potts antiferromagnet) have a **2+2-dimensional height**: each edge carries `B = t ⊗ E` (`t` a fixed orientation, `E ∈ {α/2, β/2, γ/2}` by the colour pair's `V₄`-difference, `α + β + γ = 0` in a charge plane), the integral around every defect-free triangle vanishes, so the height is a well-defined map into a triangular lattice, and **every four-colouring is a fixed four-colouring of the height lattice composed with the height** (their Fig. 7); defects carry vector charges. Equivalent to Baxter's three-edge-colouring of the honeycomb (Kondev–Henley's fully packed loops). They simulate on the projective plane because the cluster algorithm is not ergodic on the torus. **For FS-79:** a four-colour height exists when the relation cycles include triangles, so G71/T29's "no height at four colours" is a statement about square relations (the grid; `[CGHO2025coh]`), not about every generating set. The hexagonal lead of `plans/STUB-algebraic-explanation.md`; de Grey's lattice contains `ℤ[ζ₆]`; ℚ(√167)'s lattices have no triangles (G57) |
| **[Cepas2016]** O. Cépas, *Colorings of odd or even chirality on hexagonal lattices*, arXiv:1611.02925 (2016) **[found as a work citing `[MooreNewman2000]` in Semantic Scholar, FS-79 gate, 2026-09-23]** | **Abstract read.** Two classes of colourings of hexagonal lattices, of odd and even chirality; the parity is invariant under every loop (Kempe) move, which is why standard Monte Carlo is non-ergodic. **For FS-79:** a ℤ/2 chirality invariant of colourings under Kempe dynamics — the physics side of Hubai's "one chirality choice" (`[Hubai2018]`) and of Coxeter's chirality reading (FS73 §8); an invariant of Kempe classes, not of existence |
| **[CepasAkhmetiev2020]** O. Cépas, P. M. Akhmetiev, *Topological interpretation of color exchange invariants: hexagonal lattice on a torus*, arXiv:2008.12689 (2020) | **Abstract read (FS-79 gate).** Kempe (colour-exchange) invariants of a 2-D colouring problem are polynomials in winding numbers, identified with linking numbers in 3-D and an Arf–Kervaire invariant. **For FS-79:** the discipline's evidence that invariants of colourings under recolouring moves can be higher-topological (linking, not only winding); bears on Kempe classes (`[MoharSalas2009]`), not directly on existence |
| **[Freedman1998]** M. H. Freedman, *k-SAT on groups and undecidability*, STOC 1998; zbMATH 1028.68068 | **Author's summary read (zbMATH).** An infinite generalisation of 2-SAT (decidable) and 3-SAT (undecidable) on groups; as a corollary, **3-colourability of doubly periodic planar graphs is undecidable**. Several vertex orbits, like `[Burr1984]`; our class (Cayley graphs, one orbit) is decidable at three colours (FS73-T22), so the undecidability does not transfer by itself |
| **[Bitar2023]** N. Bitar, *Contributions to the domino problem: seeding, recurrence and satisfiability*, PhD thesis, arXiv:2312.08911 (2023) | **Abstract read (FS-79 gate).** Seeded and recurring domino problems on finitely generated groups (invariant under change of generating set), and a `k`-SAT problem on groups: the domino problem reduces to 3-SAT for scalable groups (ℤ^d is scalable). SAT on a group is not graph colouring with a fixed target, so it does not settle FS-79's class; the nearest place a reduction of the domino problem to Cayley-graph colouring would appear |

A triangle-free universal target for a triangle-free class — for **planar** graphs, and our lattice graphs are not planar. Both FS-79 competitors declined the held item it supported |

Verify the statement and the citation before anything leans on it |

**FS-85 input audit's prior-art gate (2026-09-24; `plans/FS85-input-audit.md` § A3).** Three debts: the periodicity of all four-colourings of `R₂` / de Grey's lattice (the wiki page, the threads, zbMATH), triangle-free uniquely `k`-colourable circulants for every `k`, and a triangle-free distance graph with a forced pair. The zbMATH queries, verbatim with their hits, are `data/fs85/audit/zbmath_queries.py`.

|---|---|
| **[Nesetril1973]** *On uniquely colorable graphs without short cycles* (J. Nešetřil; Čas. Pěst. Mat. 98 (1973) 122–125; doi 10.21136/CPM.1973.108481, Zbl 0257.05107; DML-CZ) | **Read in full** (held). Theorem 2: a **triangle-free uniquely (k+2)-colourable graph for every k ≥ 1**, built on a long path with one new vertex per chosen independent set — not vertex-transitive; Theorem 1 finds the uniquely n-colourable graphs of maximum degree ≤ n (exactly two). He conjectures the version without cycles of length ≤ g; titles proving it, seen as zbMATH listings only: Bollobás–Sauer 1976 (*Uniquely colourable graphs with large girth*), Zhu 1996 (uniquely H-colourable, large girth), Emden-Weinert–Hougardy–Kreuter 1998 (explicit bounds). "Triangle-free uniquely k-colourable for every k" is his; the **circulant** version is not |
| **[OravcovaSotak2024]** *On a problem concerning integer distance graphs* (J. Oravcová, R. Soták; arXiv:2401.12347) | **Read: §1, theorem statements, references** (held). Quotes **Zhu's conjecture** (`[Zhu2002]` Conj. 4.1): a triangle-free integer distance graph `G(ℤ, D)`, `|D| ≥ 3`, has `χ ≤ |D|`; and the `|D| = 3` classification (`χ = 4` only for `{1, 2, 3n}` or `{x, y, x+y}`, both with triangles). Answers "does `χ = |D|+1` force `ω ≥ |D|`?" negatively. Its Thm 2.2 uses a forced-period argument at the maximal colour count, with triangles present. Nothing on forced pairs in triangle-free distance graphs |

**The consolidation run's reference-list mining (2026-09-27, job LP; `data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lp/record.md`)** — `[Johnson2006]`, `[Johnson2000]`,
`[BendaPerles2000]`. Each title below is **[title from a reference list]** — seen only in the list of the paper named, its metadata checked against zbMATH the same day — with the
verdict the citing paper's own text supports. References that already had an entry were updated in place.

| title (authors; venue; source) | verdict |
|---|---|
| **[AbramsJohnson2001]** *Yet another species of forbidden-distances chromatic number* (A. Abrams, P. Johnson; Geombinatorics 10 (January 2001) 89–95; Zbl 0979.05051) — `[Johnson2006]` [1] | **Listing only.** Introduces the Babai numbers `B_k(V)` and shows `B₁(ℚ²) = 2` (`[Johnson2006]` p. 215); `ℚⁿ` only; not held |
| **[Chilakamarri1990]** *On the chromatic number of rational five-space* (K. B. Chilakamarri; Aequationes Math. 39 (1990) 146–148; Zbl 0705.05034) — `[Johnson2006]` [4], `[Johnson2000]` [4] | **Listing only.** `χ(ℚ⁵) ≥ 6` (`[Johnson2000]` p. 176), improved by Mann (`[Johnson2006]` p. 210); not held |
| **[Chilakamarri1993]** *The unit-distance graph problem: a brief survey and some new results* (K. B. Chilakamarri; Bull. ICA 8 (1993) 39–60; Zbl 0803.05021) — `[Johnson2006]` [5] | **Review read earlier (FS-81's gate, above): negative for quadratic fields.** Johnson found Schoenberg's clique theorem through it (p. 216); not held |
| **[FranklWilson1981]** *Intersection theorems with geometric consequences* (P. Frankl, R. M. Wilson; Combinatorica 1(4) (1981) 357–368; Zbl 0498.05048) — `[Johnson2006]` [9], `[Johnson2000]` [10] | **Listing only.** `χ(ℝⁿ) ≥ (1 + o(1))(1.2)ⁿ` and a `ℚⁿ` form (`[Johnson2006]` p. 210: best `ρ ≈ 1.13`); dimension growth, not the plane; not held |
| **[Johnson1982]** *Coloring Abelian groups* (P. D. Johnson Jr.; Discrete Math. 40 (1982) 219–223, received 9 January 1981; Zbl 0485.05009; doi 10.1016/0012-365X(82)90122-4) — `[Johnson2006]` [10], `[Johnson1990]` [3] | **Read in full (2026-09-27, job LX).** Held: `library/pdf/johnson-1982-coloring-abelian-groups-discrete-math-40-219-223.pdf` (Kooshi's browser download; a scan with a noisy text layer, so every statement here was read from the page images). **What it proves, verbatim.** p. 219: *"An n-coloring of G will be a partition of G into n sets."*; *"An n-coloring of G is h-alternating if and only if, for each g ∈ G, the group elements g + mh, 0 ≤ m ≤ n − 1, are all colored differently."*; *"A subset S ⊆ G is weakly n-free if and only if Σ_{h∈S} m_h h = 0 implies Σ_{h∈S} m_h ≡ 0 (mod n)."* pp. 219–220: *"In the case n = 2, this last observation may be extended: if S ⊆ G, then for any S-alternating two-coloring, g and g + Σ_{h∈S} m_h h have the same color if and only if Σ_{h∈S} m_h is even."* — every proper two-colouring is the parity map on each coset, what `[Johnson2006]` p. 213 cites it for. p. 220, Proposition: *"A two-coloring B, R of G is {h, z}-alternating if and only if B is h-forbidden and R is z-forbidden."* **Theorem** (p. 220): *"Suppose S ⊆ G, and n > 1. If S is weakly n-free, then there is an S-alternating n-coloring of G. If n = 2, or if S is a singleton, the converse holds; the existence of an S-alternating n-coloring implies that S is weakly n-free."* Its proof colours `H = ⟨S⟩` by *"B_k = {Σ_{h∈S} m_h h \| Σ_{h∈S} m_h ≡ k (mod n)}, k = 0, . . . , n − 1"* and *"Extend the coloring to all of G by coloring the co-sets of H similarly."* Corollary 1 (p. 220): *"Suppose S₁ and S₂ are non-empty subsets of G. There is a two coloring B; R of G such that B is S₁-forbidden and R is S₂-forbidden if and only if S₁ ∪ S₂ is weakly two-free."* **Corollary 2** (p. 220): *"Suppose s > 0. There is a two-coloring of the real line (with red and blue, say), such that no two red points are 1 apart, and no two blue points are s apart, if and only if s ∉ K = {p/q \| p, q are positive integers and p + q is odd}."* — on the rationals `s ∉ K` ⟺ `v₂(s) = 0`, the one-dimensional two-colour prototype of the `v₂ = 0` answers (`[JungreisReidWitte1990]`, `[Chow1993]`, and job LX's ℚ⁴ law at four colours). **Corollary 3** (p. 221): *"The chromatic number of Q² and Q³ is 2, and of Q⁴ is greater than 2."*, ℚ⁴ by the odd relation *"3 · [⅙(1, 1, 3, 5)] − 1 · [½(1, 1, 1, 1)] − (0, 0, 1, 0) − 2(0, 0, 0, 1) = (0, 0, 0, 0)"* (checked: `data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lx/johnson1982_checks.py`). Remarks (pp. 221–222): (i) the colouring for `s = p/q`, `p, q` odd, by intervals `[n/q, (n+1)/q)`; (ii) a weakly 2-free `S ⊆ (0, ∞)` with *"x² + y² = 1, x, y ≥ 0 implies x ∈ S or y ∈ S"* would 4-colour the plane by a product colouring — *"However, the author does not know whether or not such an S exists."* (No such `S` exists: the product colouring would contradict `χ(ℝ²) ≥ 5`, `[deGrey2018]`, rung B; job LX); (iv) the scheme of `k` colours each with its own forbidden distances, and problems (a) and (b). **Bearing (job LX, `data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lx/record.md` stage 2): it holds Theorem A's metric-free form IN PART.** It has the setting — any abelian group, any set `S` of differences, the colouring of `⟨S⟩` copied to its cosets — in print from 1982, before `[Fischer1990]`. Its target is `ℤ/n`, and its colouring is the homomorphism `Σ m_h h ↦ Σ m_h mod n` (written as the partition `B_k`, never named), equal to 1 on `S`. At `n = 2` that is Theorem A's case `|G| = 2` in full, with a converse and the uniqueness of the colouring that Theorem A lacks. For `n ≥ 3`, weak `n`-freeness excludes every pair `h, −h`, and the colouring maps the graph into the `n`-cycle, so it never colours a graph of chromatic number ≥ 4: not Theorem B, not the finite-plane bound, not row 57's ℚ⁴ residue. The arbitrary target is `[Fischer1990]` Thm 1(i) and `[Johnson2006]` p. 213 (Euclidean). References: `[ErdosEtAl1973]` [1], `[Woodall1973]` [2]. |
| **[JohnsonLee2006]** *Two theorems concerning the Babai numbers* (P. Johnson, A. Lee; Geombinatorics 15 (2006) 177–182; Zbl 1506.05071; "submitted" in `[Johnson2006]` [13]) | **Listing only.** Babai numbers of `ℤⁿ`, `ℚⁿ` (`[Johnson2006]` pp. 215–216); not a field plane; not held |
| **[LarmanRogers1972]** *The realization of distances within sets in Euclidean space* (D. G. Larman, C. A. Rogers; Mathematika 19 (1972) 1–24; Zbl 0246.05020) — `[Johnson2006]` [15] | **Listing only.** The `(3 + o(1))ⁿ` upper bound for `χ(ℝⁿ)` (`[Johnson2006]` p. 215); not held |
| **[Mann2001]** *A new bound for the chromatic number of the rational five-space* (M. Mann; Geombinatorics 11 (October 2001) 49–53; Zbl 0995.05051) — `[Johnson2006]` [16] | **Listing only.** `χ(ℚ⁵) ≥ 7` (`[Johnson2006]` p. 210); not held |
| **[Mann2003]** *Hunting unit-distance graphs in rational n-spaces* (M. Mann; Geombinatorics 13 (October 2003) 86–97; Zbl 1054.05041) — `[Johnson2006]` [17] | **Listing only.** `χ(ℚ⁶) ≥ 10`, `χ(ℚ⁷) ≥ 13`, `χ(ℚ⁸) ≥ 16` by search (`[Johnson2006]` p. 210); `ℚⁿ` only; not held |
| **[Schoenberg1937]** *Regular simplices and quadratic forms* (I. J. Schoenberg; J. London Math. Soc. 12 (1937) 48–55; Zbl 0015.39103) — `[Johnson2006]` [18] | **Listing only.** When the maximum clique number of `G(ℤⁿ, d)` is `n` or `n + 1` (`[Johnson2006]` p. 216); not held |
| **[Soifer1991]** *Chromatic number of the plane: a historical essay* (A. Soifer; Geombinatorics 1 (1991) 13–15; Zbl 0853.05038) — `[Johnson2006]` [19] | **Listing only.** History; not held |
| M. A. Trick, *Network resources for coloring a graph*, `http://mat.gsia.cmu.edu/COLOR/color.html` — `[Johnson2006]` [20] | **Listing only.** The colouring program behind Mann's bounds; a web page, not fetched |
| **[Zaks1999]** *On odd integral distances rational graph in four-dimensions* (J. Zaks; Geombinatorics IX(2) (October 1999) 90–95, the printed title *On Odd Integral Distances Rational Graph in four-dimensions*; Zbl 0940.05025) — `[Johnson2006]` [24] | **Read in full 2026-09-27 (job LV, pp. 90–95 from 150-dpi renders).** `Q^d(odd)`: rational points, edges at odd integer distance. **Theorem 1** (p. 90): `Q²(odd)`, `Q³(odd)` have infinitely many components; **Corollary 1** (p. 91): odd-distance sets in `ℚ²`, `ℚ³` have at most two points; **Theorem 2** (p. 91): `Q⁴(odd)` has infinitely many components. **Lemma 1** (p. 92), verbatim: *"In every proper four coloring of Q⁴(1), every two rational points at distance √2 have the same color."* — the unit `K₄` `A = (1/2, 0, 0, 1/2)`, `B = (1/2, 0, 0, −1/2)`, `C = (0, 1/2, 1/2, 0)`, `D = (0, 1/2, −1/2, 0)` and `E = (−1/2, 0, 0, −1/2)` adjacent to `B, C, D` force `φ(E) = φ(A)` at distance `√2` (p. 91; the print's second *"φ(B) = 4"* means `φ(D)`), then a rational reflection moves the pair (p. 92) — checked by hand here. **Lemma 2** (p. 92): *"In every proper four coloring of Q⁴(1), every two rational points at distance 2 have the same color."* — two proofs, the first needing `[Johnson2006]` p. 212's correction `D = (1, 1, 0, 0)` (checked: `D` must be at `√2` from `(0,0,0,0)` and `(2,0,0,0)`), the second by the rational similarity of ratio `√2` (Perles's idea, a 4×4 matrix). **Theorem 3** (p. 93): *"In every proper four coloring of Q⁴(1), every two rational points at odd distance have different colors, hence χ(Q⁴(odd)) = 4."* **Corollary 2**: at most four rational points of `ℚ⁴` with odd mutual distances. **Open Problem** (p. 93): the maximum `m(d)` of rational points in `ℚ^d`, `d ≥ 5`, with odd mutual distances; `w_d ≤ m(d) ≤ χ(Q^d(odd))` (p. 94). Colour classes dense (p. 94, *"as in [11]"*, `[Zaks1989]`). **Pre-empts nothing of ours**: `LEDGER.md` row 59 is the plane over K and states the valuation law's necessary direction; row 57's quaternion remark reproves Benda–Perles's `χ(ℚ⁴) ≤ 4`. **It answers, in `ℚ⁴`, the sufficiency row 59 leaves open over K**, at `√2`, 2 (forced equal) and odd integers (forced different) — and with Benda–Perles's `[H⁴ : D] = 2` and the quaternion residue (row 57) it gives the whole law, LV-T3 (`data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lv/record.md`, [derived, one mind]): a pair is forced equal in every proper four-colouring of `G(ℚ⁴, 1)` iff `v₂(\|x − y\|²) ≥ 1`, forced different iff `= 0`, free iff `< 0`; so a four-colouring forbidding 1 and `d` exists iff `d² = p/q`, `p, q` odd — `[Johnson2006]` p. 212's open question (`CLOSED-QUESTIONS.md` §3). References (p. 94–95) held or listed but three, not entered (odd-distance sets, no bearing): Graham–Rothschild–Straus, *Amer. Math. Monthly* 81 (1974) 21–25; Rosenfeld, Geombinatorics 5 (1996) 156–159; Rosenfeld, *The Mathematics of Paul Erdős* (1997) 318–323. Its [12] gives `[Zaks1992]` as Ars Combin. 33 (1992) **307–311**, against the 253–256 of `[Johnson2006]` [23] and of `[Zaks1992]`'s entry — unresolved. |
| **[ChilakamarriMahoney1995]** *Maximal and minimal forbidden unit-distance graphs in the plane* (K. B. Chilakamarri, C. R. Mahoney; Bull. ICA 13 (1995) 35–43; Zbl 0843.05055) — `[Johnson2000]` [5] | **Listing only.** Graphs one edge from unit-distance (`[Johnson2000]` p. 173); not `[ChilakamarriMahoney1996]`; not held |

| **[ErdosEtAl1973]** *Euclidean Ramsey theorems. I* (P. Erdős, R. L. Graham, P. Montgomery, B. L. Rothschild, J. Spencer, E. G. Straus; J. Combin. Theory Ser. Davies, *Measurable obstructions for unmeasurable colourings*, arXiv:2610.12301 (v1, 8 October 2026), 47 pp. | **Read in full** (2026-10-13, FS131-ER1, `plans/FS131-ER1-davies-reading.md`): pp. 1–37 and Appendices A–B from the rendered pages, the references as text; the proofs of Lemma B.1, Lemma B.2 and of Theorem 2.2's converse checked line by line; §§ 4–6 and 8–10 read, not re-derived; the supplement `[48]` unread. **Proves** (Thm 1.1, p. 3): for finite `D` with rational squares, `ᾱ_D(ℝ⁴) = m_D(ℝ⁴)`, `χ_D(ℝ⁴) = χ_D^(m)(ℝ⁴)`, and for `d ⩾ 5` the same with `ℚ^d`; `χ(ℝ⁴) = χ(ℚ(√n)⁴)` for some non-square `n` (not exhibited); `(2 + o(1))^d ⩽ χ(ℝ^d) ⩽ (3√3/2 + o(1))^d` (the abstract's `3√3/4` is a slip). `χ^(m)` is Falconer's number: Lebesgue-measurable classes, proper at every pair. Its certificate theorems (2.1, 2.2) hold for `d ⩾ 2`; its transference is four-dimensional (Magyar's bound, quaternions). **Poses** (p. 5): whether `ℚ(√n)²`, `n ≡ 47, 143, 167 (mod 168)`, is six-colourable or holds a five-chromatic unit-distance graph (rows 121, 123 answer the second, given MR); Conjecture 1.6, `χ(ℝ²) = 6`; "In the plane we suspect that a more complicated extension of ℚ² might be required" — a suspicion, no argument (against row 128). **Its Lemma B.1** (p. 44: measurable avoiding sets with conull union give a measurable proper colouring) closes row 130's null-set step; with `[OAIMath2026]` Thm 1.3 it gives `χ(ℝ²) = χ_m(ℝ²)`, which the paper does not draw (FS131-ER1 § M, one mind). Does not use `[OAIMath2026]` (cited once, p. 37). Unrefereed, version 1. Held: `pdf/Davies2026-measurable-obstructions-unmeasurable-colourings.pdf` (sha256 `58e7154a…`). |

## G. The Geombinatorics archive walk (2026-09-27)

The archive walk's files (`data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lw/`) are in the project's document library under `geombinatorics-from-lean-dir/` since FS131-PS2, at the same relative paths; the paths below are their old repository paths.

*Job LW of the consolidation run (`data/consolidation-2026-09-27/briefs/LW-archive-walk.md`; record `data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lw/record.md`,
verbatim notes `data/consolidation-2026-09-27/lw/reading-notes.md`). The 44 held recent issues, vol. XXV no. 2 (October 2015) to vol. XXXVI
no. 1 (July 2026), are `library/geombinatorics/geombinatorics-v<vol>-n<no>-<yyyy>-<mm>.pdf`, catalogued article by article in
`library/geombinatorics/CATALOGUE.md` (cross-checked with zbMATH's 173 records). XXXIII(4) (April 2024) is not held: two downloads through the
fetcher failed. XXV(2)–XXIX(1) are image scans with no text layer; XXX(2), XXX(3) and XXXIV(4) have unreadable text layers. One row per article
read or flagged; an existing key is cited here, not redefined, and its § A or § C row stays the authority for everything but the journal
copy's file.*

**Read** (verbatim statements with pages in the notes):

| key | work | what bears | file | read |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Polymath, *On the chromatic number of circular disks and infinite strips in the plane*, Geombinatorics XXX(4) (April 2021) 190–201 (Zbl 1466.05215) | **FS-112's question, posed:** p. 199, strengthen `[Thomassen1999]` by eliminating "(ii) the unit-radius tactic"; p. 194 defines the tactic (a boundary arc of radius 1 centred on a point where a same-coloured tile touches); the record six-tiling of a strip (height √3 + √15/2, `[AxenovichEtAl2014]` Thm 4.2) uses it (p. 194; Fig. 6 caption, p. 196: "The sloping boundaries are arcs of radius 1") — a known positive for FS-112's checkers; the six-tiled disk (radius ≈ 2.12426, Fig. 10, p. 200) shows curved boundaries, but the text does not say they are radius-1 arcs and gives no coordinates, so it is no exact positive as printed (LW2). Also prints Harder's `8/3` forced pair (pp. 192–193) and Parts's `8/9` pair (p. 198; the 31,171-vertex disk graph p. 197). Polymath, *Probabilistic formulation of Hadwiger-Nelson problem*, Polymath Wiki, last edited 7 May 2019; `michaelnielsen.org/polymath/index.php?title=Probabilistic_formulation_of_Hadwiger-Nelson_problem` (the live site is gone; read through the Internet Archive snapshot of 2026-05-12). Not a Geombinatorics item: read for `[GwynStavrianos2022]`'s reference [7] | the averaging device of link 2's argument, from a colouring assumed proper: "Suppose for sake of contradiction that we have a 4-coloring [...] with no unit edges monochromatic", then a left-invariant finitely additive probability on `S_4 × E(2)` makes a stationary random colouring ("an example of the Furstenberg correspondence principle"); correlation bounds `p_d` (Lemmas 1–40, Propositions 35–36). No badness of an improper colouring, no Lemma-2 inequality. **Lemmas 1–40** (LW3): correlation inequalities for that process, each from a finite configuration by a finite union bound (Lemma 3's proof: "With probability at least 1 − 52 P(…), none of these triangles are monochromatic"), the contradictions coming from non-4-colourable graphs ("Computer-verified Claim 5", "6", "11"); Lemma 2 ("Spindle argument", `p_d ≤ 1/2` for `|d| ≥ 1/2`) and Lemmas 30–32 hold at any number of colours. They hold verbatim for a countably additive stationary colouring proper a.s. at unit pairs, so they are the easy direction `χ_fin ≤ χ_CP` and a template for FS-104's correlation-constraint route; row 130's hard direction (a measurable a.e.-proper colouring gives a CP one) and row 131's band and holes do not appear. Theorem 20's proof (`p_{H=1tri} > 0`, a sketch) classifies the four-colourings of `ℤ[e^{πi/3}]` with no "1tri" hexagon into seven cases, stripes among them (Cases 4–6) — a neighbouring statement to row 106's stripe lemma under a weaker hypothesis, beside `[Parts2020hv]` §3 (LW3, one mind). Kainen, *Is the chromatic number of the plane robust?*, Geombinatorics 2(2) (October 1992) 33–36 (Zbl 0846.05029) | **Poses a conjecture that is the bold form.** p. 35: "(2) CONJECTURE. The chromatic number of the plane is robust." — p. 34: "The robustness of f for (M,r) is largest u for which f(G(M, (−u + r, u + r))) = f(G(M,r)) [...] f is robust if it has positive robustness." p. 34, Theorem 1: Isbell's colouring colours the band `(1 − u, 1 + u)` for `u ≤ (√7 − 2)/(√7 + 2)` — the upper half of `[Voronov2023]` Cor. 1.1, in print in 1992. As printed its large squares have side 1 and so contain unit pairs; the correction (3(3) (1994) 102) is unread. Then there exist two points a unit distance apart in the same set."; p. 112, Theorem 9: for any five sets covering the plane, the set of omitted distances "is totally disconnected"; p. 113: "In [1] Erdős poses the following question: [...] what is the chromatic number of G, denoted h(2, α, β)? Shelah, *Examples on the plane and Rⁿ*, Geombinatorics XIII(4) (April 2004) 186–192 (Zbl 1057.03037); the summary of `[SoiferShelah2004]` (JCTA 105) | p. 189: `G₄` on ℝ², edges `s − t − ε ∈ ℚ²` for `ε = (√2,0), (0,√2), (√2,√2), (−√2,√2)`; "Claim 1: In ZFC the chromatic number of G4 is equal to 4."; p. 190: "Claim 2: In ZF + AC_ℵ0 + LM the chromatic number of the graph G4 cannot be equal to any positive integer n nor even to ℵ0."; the same for `G₂`, `G₃` (two and three `ε`), and p. 190, "Our reasoning and results would not change if we were to replace √2 everywhere with another irrational number". Claims; proofs in the JCTA paper. **Reading (LW3, one mind):** these translate graphs have a connection set accumulating at 0, so by continuity of translation in L¹ they have no countable measurable colouring even modulo null sets of edges; the unit circle is bounded away from 0 and carries a Rajchman measure, the difference `[Payne2009]`'s and G81's ambiguity turns on, and why rows 128 and 130 can hold for the plane | `library/geombinatorics/geombinatorics-v13-n4-2004-04.pdf` (sheets 14–17) | **read in full** (LW3) |
| **[Delahaye2006]** | J.-P. Delahaye, *Imaginary coloring*, translated from the French by S. Cook, Geombinatorics XV(3) (January 2006) 101–119 (Zbl 1098.05029, as Delahaye and Cook); first published as *Coloriages irréels*, Pour la Science, February 2005, 88–93 (p. 101, footnote 1) | a popular exposition of `[ShelahSoifer2003]`'s line graph `G₁` with the two proofs sketched (p. 116: "Result 1 With AC, it is possible to color G1 with two colors"; "Result 2 Using DC + LM, we establish that no coloring with n colors is possible for G1"). p. 109: "If the chromatic number for Nelson's graph really depends on the axiom of choice, as is the case for A. Soifer and S. Shelah's graphs, a mathematician will be hard-pressed to maintain that foundational questions [...] are unimportant." No theorem of its own; no bearing beyond the question it relays | `library/geombinatorics/geombinatorics-v15-n3-2006-01.pdf` (sheets 3–12) | **read in full** (LW3) |
| `[Soifer2007]` (§ A) | A. Soifer, *Chromatic number of the plane theorem in Solovay's set theory*, Geombinatorics XVII(2) (October 2007) 85–87 (Zbl 1506.03106) | p. 85: "In the Solovay System of Axioms for set theory the chromatic number of the plane is between 5 and 7."; p. 86: "ZFS will stand for the Solovay System of Axioms, which consists of Zermelo-Fraenkel System of Axioms with the addition of the Principle of Dependent Choices and the axiom stating that every set of real numbers is Lebesgue measurable"; "Chromatic Number of the Plane Theorem. In ZFS the chromatic number of the plane is 5, 6, or 7." No proof printed: it is `[Falconer1981]`'s `χ_m ≥ 5` in a model where every set is measurable (Falconer is not cited), with an inaccessible cardinal assumed (footnote 1). Since `[deGrey2018]` the ZFC value is also in {5, 6, 7}, so the note no longer separates the two systems. **Bearing (LW3, a reading):** in ZF + DC + LM the plane's number is `χ_m` computed there, and restriction to finite subgraphs gives the ZFC value (`χ_fin`, an arithmetic number) as a lower bound in any model; the random-motion half of link 2 (`χ_fin ≤ χ_m⁰`) uses only countable additivity (ZF + DC), and link 2's use of choice sits in de Bruijn–Erdős alone. So the question the cluster poses is exactly `χ_m = χ_fin` in ZFS: given MR, `χ_m⁰ = χ_fin` (rows 128, 130), and what is left is the null-set step and its choice-free form — FS-104 items (1) and (3) | `library/geombinatorics/geombinatorics-v17-n2-2007-10.pdf` (sheets 19–20) | **read in full** (LW3) |
| **[Johnson1994choice]** | P. D. Johnson Jr., *The choice number of the plane*, Geombinatorics 3(4) (April 1994) 122–128 (Zbl 0848.05031) | **Poses** (p. 124) "Is the choice number of the planar unit distance graph finite?" and proves, p. 125, item 1: "The well-known DeBruijn-Erdös theorem on the chromatic number of an infinite graph holds for the choice number. That is, if G is an infinite graph, k is a positive integer, and c(H) ≤ k for every finite subgraph H of G, then c(G) ≤ k" (by Tychonoff; the proof runs for any finite lists); item 2, the Benda–Perles–Tarski reduction for the choice number (pp. 125–126); item 4, the rational plane has choice number at least 3 (p. 126). **Bears on FS-104 item (1)** (row 130's null-set step, list-colouring the metrical boundary from lists of size ≥ 2, "a question about finite configurations"): item 1 is that step's compactness, in print, and it uses choice (LW3). Whether its question is answered in print is a debt (a candidate, recalled and not read: an average-degree lower bound for choice numbers, which would make the plane's choice number infinite and exclude a generic choosability argument for item (1)) | `library/geombinatorics/geombinatorics-v03-n4-1994-04.pdf` (sheets 10–13) | **read in full** (LW3) |

## D. Standing rules for this file

0. **The prior-art gate is a workflow step** (`WORKFLOW.md` step 3.2, `CLAUDE.md` § Grokkings G49). Every milestone
   runs a thirty-minute prior-art search at its OPENING, before any derivation, and records the result as a §PA
   section of its subplan — searching in the *discipline's* vocabulary and never in this project's coined one,
   under at least two disciplines for the same object, for the QUESTION as well as the method, and outside arXiv.
   Anything found lands in this file the same day. *Why:* four milestones (M58–M61) reconstructed
   `[Moorhouse2010]`, which was one web search away, while `[CervantesKrebs2023]` sat unread in § C systematizing
   one of their kernel theorems.
0b. **Read status is recorded in three places, and they must agree**: the row's read column in § A, the file's
   line in § B, and the lists in rules 0d and 0e. When a work is read, obtained, or found unobtainable, all three
   change in the same edit.
0c. **A § C row is a LIABILITY, not an archive.** A § C title whose one-line guess touches a live milestone is
   fetched and scanned before that milestone opens, not after it closes — minutes, because the guess is already
   written down. The same holds for a named-only row in § A that touches live work. *Why:* four § C titles turned

   hand-derived a special case of it), `[SokolovVoronov2025]` (carrying the theorem that subsumes M37–M40's floor,
   under a note that correctly guessed so), `[deGreyParts2022]` and `[PartsOdd2022]`.

The paste-ready filing notes, with every identifier, holder and route and the sources for

0f. **Log every work the day it is first used, and mine the reference list of every paper read the same day**
   (`CLAUDE.md` G50). When a work is read, every title in its bibliography that sits in our neighbourhood goes into
   § A as a **named-only row immediately**, with one line saying why it might matter. *Why:* the chain that found
   the seventh lineage was four references deep (`[Parts2023]` → `[deGreyParts2022]`; `[DuczVarga2026]` →
   `[Ducz2026]` → the Polymath16 wiki), and `[Heule2021]`, found this way, is the closest published work to a
   theorem proved here the day before. A citation list read the day the paper is read costs ten minutes; the same
   list read a year late costs a retracted novelty claim.

   once dismissed by its abstract while its conclusion asked the question Theorem B answers. **Look outside the
   indexes**: the seventh lineage is a wiki page that no citation database lists and that 404s on its own site. Anything
   found outside an index is archived to `library/notes/` the same day, as `[Polymath16wiki]` and
   `[Polymath16threads]` are.
0h. **`python3 tools/bib_audit.py` reports zero unlogged works, and `--self-test` fires its three controls.** It
   walks every `.md` in the repository, extracts arXiv ids and bracketed citation keys, and checks each against a
   bolded row key or the alternate-key table in § A′. **Run it at every milestone close, beside the Frontier
   update.** *Why:* prose is not an index — works named only in `plans/` stay unlogged, and a rule enforced by
   memory is not enforced. § A′ exists because the script's first version could not see a key merged into another
   entry, and the self-test requires a planted gap to be seen.

## E. Glossary — this project's vocabulary against the literature's

*Why this exists.* `CLAUDE.md` § Grokkings **G49** names a private vocabulary as **the mechanism that hid the
prior art**: a working term coined here appears in no paper, so a search in our words filters out the literature
that uses the discipline's words. Citation-database searches did not reach `[Polymath16wiki]`; the search that
found `[Vinh2006]` used *the discipline's* phrase, not ours.

*The decision (Kooshi, 2026-09-09):* **"we should not wholly rewrite our history with the terms of the
literature, but we should replace any where the scope is small and our chosen term might lead to confusion."**
So: **a translation table, adoption of the literature's term in all NEW writing and in the paper, targeted
replacement where the term is both narrow and confusable, and closed milestone records left alone** — a
milestone's record is what we believed when we believed it, and `T13` keeps its title.

| ours | the literature's | source | decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| **the direction ladder** (T13) | **the Moser lattice** `ℤ⟨1, ζ₆, ω₃, ζ₆ω₃⟩`, `ω₃ = (5+i√11)/6` | `[MatolcsiEtAl2025]` Def. 1, `[Ducz2026]`, `[Aggarwal2025]` — **three independent sources** | **Adopted (2026-09-09).** Narrow (25 uses) and genuinely confusable — "ladder" also names M24's *matched ladder pair*. New writing says **Moser lattice**; tool output updated; `STANDING-QUESTIONS.md`'s T13 keeps its title with a gloss |
| **the seam field** `K` | `ℚ(√3,√11)`; the ring `ℤ[ζ₆, (1+√33)/2, ⅓]` is the **Moser spindle ring** `R̄₂` | `[Polymath16wiki]`; `[Madore2015]` Prop. 4.6 writes *"If K = ℚ(√3, √11), then 4 ≤ χ(K²) ≤ 5"*, `K` being his letter for the field under study throughout (checked in the held PDF, 2026-09-25) — the earliest held use of `K` for this field | **Say the field.** In the paper, `K` defined once and never nicknamed |
| **de Grey's lattice** `Λ = (1/9)O_E`, `E = ℚ(√−3,√−11)`, its 54 unit vectors `ζ₆^a η^b`, `\|b\| ≤ 4` (FS-70 on; rows 78, 84–89) | **no literature name found**; the nearest is the **Moser lattice** `ℤ⟨1, ζ₆, ω₃, ζ₆ω₃⟩` above, which is **not** the same object (row 85's scope note; FS-88) | **ours, and the attribution is not de Grey's:** `[deGrey2018]` (arXiv:1804.02385 v3, text checked 2026-09-25) defines no lattice and names no field — he builds graphs `H, J, K, L, M, N` from hexagons and Moser spindles, and his rotations by `2 arcsin(1/4)` and `2 arcsin(1/8)` put his graph in `K(√5, √7) = ℚ(√3,√5,√7,√11)`, which is what this project calls **de Grey's field** (G43). He also names a *graph* `K`, a collision with our field `K`. Coined in the owner's FS-70 notes (2026-09-22) | **Decision owed (Kooshi):** the owner's proposal is to keep the name in closed records (policy above: not narrow, 100+ uses), and in new writing to say *"the rank-4 lattice `Λ = (1/9)O_E` with 54 unit vectors"* and credit its pieces to their sources (the Moser spindle, `[Polymath16wiki]`'s unit group `⟨ζ₆, η⟩`, Hubai's ring computation). In chat reports the owner twice called `K` itself "de Grey's field"; that was wrong — `K` is the Moser spindle's field |
| **the seam plane** | `K²` | — | **Say `K²`.** Four prose uses; the Lean identifier `seamPlane` stays (renaming it costs a rebuild and buys nothing) |
| **virtual edge** | **`[Heule2019]`'s own term** — *"hence 1/3 is a so-called virtual-edge"*; `[Heule2021]` builds on it | `[Heule2019]` §5 | **KEEP — it was never ours.** But `[Parts2020a]` §2 rejects it in print: *"this term is not entirely successful, since it does not clearly express whether one means a mono- or non-mono-pair, which leads to confusion."* **He is right, and the 2-adic law is exactly about that polarity**, so the paper uses **mono-pair / non-mono-pair** |
| **forced pair**, *forced monochromatic* | **mono-pair** | `[Parts2020a]`, `[PartsOdd2022]` | **Adopt in new writing.** Theirs is shorter and states the polarity |
| **cheats**, *non-unit edges* | **`d`-edges** / *blue edges* in the two-distance literature | `[Agoston2026]` (red/blue edge-bicoloured graphs) | **Keep in the record (817 uses — not narrow), never in the paper.** New writing says *`d`-edge* |
| **spindle chord** | **rotation edge** | `[PartsOdd2022]` §1 | Adopt in new writing; ours is not wrong, theirs is published |
| **`spindleRot d`** (indexed by the **distance**) | **`ω_t` / `θ_t`, indexed by the SQUARED distance**: `ω_t = exp(i·arccos(1 − 1/2t))` | `[Polymath16wiki]`, `[Heule2019]` §2 | **HIGHEST CONFUSION RISK IN THIS TABLE.** Their `ω₄` is our `spindleRot 2`; their `ω_{64/9}` is our `spindleRot (8/3)`. A `--` note is in `Copy.lean`; always state which convention |
| **the half-spindle group** `⟨ζ₁₂, σ, √ρ₁₅, √ρ₆₃⟩` | the **unit-modulus group** of the ring, `⟨ω, η, ζ, α, β, γ⟩` with `η = (√33+i√3)/6`, `ζ = (1+i√15)/4`, **`α = (√5+i√11)/4`** | `[Polymath16wiki]`, derived by UFD factorisation, 2018 | **Theirs is earlier, derived, and has a generator we lack.** See G8 |
| **pigeonhole × kill** (G15) | *"the **large part** limits the 4-colourings of 12 vertices at distance 2 to 19; the **small part** prevents these"* | `[Heule2019]` §6.2, after `[Heule2018]` | Keep ours as the mechanism's name; cite his as the construction's own description |
| **additive colouring** (M56) | **linear colouring** on the wiki; **additive colouring** in `[Fischer1990]` | `[Fischer1990]`, `[Polymath16wiki]` | Fischer's is the published mathematical term; **use "additive"** |
| **the plane over `F`**, **the field plane**, `UD(F, 2)` | **planar geometric graph** (`[Fischer1994]`'s title and Harborth's phrase: *"a geometric planar graph G one means the collection of points in ℝ² whose coordinates lie in some field F containing the rationals"*); *the graph `F²`* in `[Fischer1990]` | `[Fischer1994]` §1 | **Keep ours in the record; the paper names his.** The term is the reason the 1994 title read as a planar-graphs paper in every listing (G49) — a search for *planar geometric graph* with *chromatic number* finds it at once |
| **the field wall**, **wall-broken**, **5-sealed**, **usable prime** | *unnamed everywhere* — `[Fischer1990]`, `[Madore2015]` and `[BardestaniMallahiKarai2015]` all state the condition (anisotropy of `x²+y²` at the prime) without naming it | — | **Keep for thinking; do NOT name it in the paper** (FS-20 §4 decision N2): naming it obscures that six authors state one condition |
| **the descent**, **the μ-dial**, **the arity floor**, **the hexagram**, **the matched ladder pair**, **the transmuter**, **the seam hexagon** | *no counterpart* | — | Ours, and they name objects nobody else has. Each keeps its name; the paper defines any it uses |
| **a point of `Θ_k`**, a **field-level slope** (G71, G74) | a **circular `k`-colouring** whose colour is a character: `x ↦ ⌊k·{θ(x)}⌋`, with `θ` a homomorphism to ℝ/ℤ putting every unit vector in `[1/k, 1 − 1/k]` | `[DeVosEtAl2007]`, `[NaserasrPujolWang2026]` (FS-81 §PA.5) | **Say "circular colouring given by a character" in new writing**; `Θ_k` stays as the set's name |
| **mono-pair at distance `d`, `v_𝔭(d) > 0`** | *odd distance* / *even distance* for integers | `[Heule2021]`, `[PartsOdd2022]`, `[Ardal2009]` | **Ours generalises theirs**: odd integers are exactly the integer case of `v_𝔭(d) = 0`. Say the valuation, cite the parity |

**One collision to know about.** `[Agoston2026]` uses *virtual* in a third, unrelated sense — a "partly virtual
edge-bicoloured graph" has green edges constrained to equal **vectors**, a construction device. Three senses of
one word across three communities; the paper should not use the bare word at all.
